Abstract
. Let μ \mu be a positive locally finite Borel measure on ℝ n \mathbb{R}^{n} that is doubling, and define the homogeneous W s ​ ( μ ) W^{s}\left(\mu\right) -Sobolev norm squared ‖ f ‖ W s ​ ( μ ) 2 \left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2} of a function f ∈ L l ​ o ​ c 2 ​ ( μ ) f\in L_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}^{2}\left(\mu\right) by ∫ ℝ n ∫ ℝ n ( f ⁡ ( x ) − f ⁡ ( y ) | x − y | s ) 2 ​ d ​ μ ​ ( x ) ​ d ​ μ ​ ( y ) | B ⁡ ( x + y 2 , | x − y | 2 ) | μ , \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)}{\left|x-y\right|^{s}}\right)^{2}\frac{d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)}{\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}}, and denote by W s ​ ( μ ) W^{s}\left(\mu\right) the corresponding Hilbert space completion (when μ \mu is Lebesgue measure, this is the familiar Sobolev space on ℝ n \mathbb{R}^{n} ). We prove in particular that for 0 ≤ α < n 0\leq\alpha<n , and σ \sigma and ω \omega doubling measures on ℝ n \mathbb{R}^{n} , there is a positive constant θ \theta such that for 0 < s < θ 0<s<\theta , the α \alpha -fractional vector Riesz transform 𝐑 α \mathbf{R}^{\alpha} is bounded from W s ​ ( σ ) W^{s}\left(\sigma\right) to W s ​ ( ω ) W^{s}\left(\omega\right) if and only if the Sobolev 𝟏 \mathbf{1} -testing and 𝟏 ∗ \mathbf{1}^{\ast} -testing conditions hold for the operator 𝐑 α \mathbf{R}^{\alpha} , i.e. ‖ 𝐑 σ α ​ 𝟏 I ‖ W s ​ ( ω ) \displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\omega\right)} ≤ \displaystyle\leq 𝔗 𝐑 α ​ ( σ , ω ) ​ | I | σ ​ ℓ ​ ( I ) − s , I ∈ 𝒬 n , \displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}, ‖ 𝐑 ω α , ∗ ​ 𝟏 I ‖ W s ​ ( σ ) ∗ \displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{R}_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\sigma\right)^{\ast}} ≤ \displaystyle\leq 𝔗 𝐑 α , ∗ ​ ( ω , σ ) ​ | I | ω ​ ℓ ​ ( I ) s , I ∈ 𝒬 n , \displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,\ast}}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\ell\left(I\right)^{s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}, taken over the family of indicator test functions { 𝟏 I } I ∈ 𝒬 n \left\{\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}} . Here 𝒬 n \mathcal{Q}^{n} is the collection of all cubes with sides parallel to the coordinate axes, and W s ​ ( μ ) ∗ W^{s}\left(\mu\right)^{\ast} denotes the dual of W s ​ ( μ ) W^{s}\left(\mu\right) determined by the usual L 2 ​ ( μ ) L^{2}\left(\mu\right) bilinear pairing, which we identify with a dyadic Sobolev space W d ​ y ​ a ​ d − s ​ ( μ ) W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right) of negative order. Under the side assumption that the classical pivotal conditions hold, the vector Riesz transform 𝐑 α \mathbf{R}^{\alpha} can be replaced by any α \alpha -fractional smooth Calderón-Zygmund operator T α T^{\alpha} .



1.
Introduction

The Nazarov-Treil-Volberg
T1
conjecture on the boundedness of the Hilbert
transform from one weighted space
L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)
to another
L^{2}\left(\omega\right)
, was settled affirmatively in the two part
paper
[
LaSaShUr3
]
,
[
Lac
]
. Since then there have been a number of
generalizations of boundedness of Calderón-Zygmund operators from one
weighted
L^{2}
space to another, both to higher dimensional Euclidean
spaces (see e.g.
[
SaShUr7
]
,
[
LaWi
]
and
[
LaSaShUrWi
]
), and
also to spaces of homogeneous type (see e.g.
[
DuLiSaVeWiYa
]
). In
addition there have been some generalizations to Sobolev spaces in place
of
L^{2}
spaces, but only in the setting of a single weight (see
e.g.
[
DiWiWI
]
and
[
KaLiPeWa
]
).

The purpose of this paper is to prove a
two weight

T1
theorem on

weighted Sobolev
spaces for vector Riesz transforms, but with
doubling
measures
1
1

1

Weighted Sobolev spaces are not canonically defined for general weights, and
doubling is a convenient hypothesis that gives equivalence of the various
definitions.
. The proof also shows that this theorem extends to smooth
\alpha
-fractional singular integrals on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, in the presence
of classical pivotal side conditions. In order to state our theorem, we need
a number of definitions, some of which are recalled and explained in detail
further below. Let
\mu
be a positive locally finite Borel measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
that is doubling, let
\mathcal{D}
be a dyadic grid on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, let
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}
be the set of weighted
Alpert projections on
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
(see
[
RaSaWi
]
). When
\kappa=1
, these are the familiar weighted Haar projections
\bigtriangleup_{Q}^{\mu}=\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}
.

Definition 1
.

Let
\mu
be a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Given
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we define the
\mathcal{D}
-dyadic homogeneous
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
-Sobolev norm of a function
f\in L_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}^{2}\left(\mu\right)
by

\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\equiv\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\ ,

and we denote by
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
the
corresponding Hilbert space completion
2
2

2

For general measures, the functional
\left\|{}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}
may only be a seminorm, but this is
avoided for doubling measures.
of
f\in L_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}^{2}\left(\mu\right)
with

\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}<\infty.

Note that
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{0}\left(\mu\right)=L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
. We will show below that
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime};\kappa^{\prime}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)

for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
with
\left|s\right|
sufficiently
small, for all
\kappa,\kappa^{\prime}\geq 1
, and for all dyadic grids
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. Thus for a sufficiently small real

s
depending on the doubling measure
\mu
, there is essentially just one
weighted ‘dyadic’ Sobolev space of order
s
, which we will denote by
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
. Moreover, for
s>0
and small
enough and
\mu
doubling, there is a more familiar equivalent ‘continuous’
norm,

(1.1)

\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\mu\right)}=\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)}{\left|x-y\right|^{s}}\right)^{2}\frac{d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)}{\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}}}.

We also show that the dual spaces
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)^{\ast}
under the
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
pairing are given by
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)
for all grids
\mathcal{D}

and integers
\kappa
. Thus when
\mu
is doubling, we can identify
W^{s}\left(\mu\right)^{\ast}
with any of the spaces
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)
for
\left|s\right|

sufficiently small, and it will be convenient to denote
W^{s}\left(\mu\right)^{\ast}
by
W^{-s}\left(\mu\right)
, even though the above
formula for
\left\|f\right\|_{W^{-s}\left(\mu\right)}
diverges
when
s\geq 0
.

Finally, we note that
without
the doubling hypothesis on
\mu
, we
in general need to include additional Haar projections
\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}^{\mu}\right\}_{T\in\mathcal{T}}
onto one-dimensional spaces of
constant functions on certain ‘tops’
T
of the grid
\mathcal{D}
, where a
top is the union of a maximal tower in
\mathcal{D}
(see
[
AlSaUr2
]
).
Without these additional projections, we may not recover all of
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
in general, and moreover, the spaces
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
defined above may actually depend on the dyadic
grid
\mathcal{D}
. For example, if
d\mu\left(x\right)=\mathbf{1}_{\left[-1,1\right)}\left(x\right)dx
and
f\left(x\right)=\mathbf{1}_{\left[0,1\right)}\left(x\right)
, the reader can easily check that the
functional
\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}
vanishes when
\mathcal{D}
is the standard dyadic grid, but is
positive when
\mathcal{D}
is any grid containing
\left[-1,1\right)
3
3

3

Moreover, even taking into account the behaviour at infinity, one can show
that
\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}=0

when
\mathcal{D}
is the standard grid and
s>0
.
.

Note that we will not use the Hilbert space duality (that identifies the
dual of a Hilbert space with itself) to analyze the two weight boundedness
of
T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}:W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)\rightarrow W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)
, but rather we will
use the
L^{2}\left(\omega\right)
and
L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)

pairings in which case the dual of
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)

is identified with
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)
as above. The
reason for this is that the weighted Alpert projections
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}
satisfy
telescoping identities, while the orthogonal projections
\left\{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}
do not.

Denote by
\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}
the collection of all dyadic grids in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and let
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
denote the collection of all
cubes in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
having sides parallel to the coordinate axes. A
positive locally finite Borel measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is said to
be doubling if there is a constant
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
, called the doubling
constant, such that

\left|2Q\right|_{\mu}\leq C_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all cubes }Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}.

For
0\leq\alpha<n
we define a smooth
\alpha
-fractional Calderón-Zygmund kernel
K^{\alpha}(x,y)
to be a function
K^{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying the following
fractional size and smoothness conditions

(1.2)

\left|\nabla_{x}^{j}K^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)\right|+\left|\nabla_{y}^{j}K^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)\right|\leq C_{\alpha,j}\left|x-y\right|^{\alpha-j-n},\ \ \ \ \ 0\leq j<\infty,

and we denote by
T^{\alpha}
the associated
\alpha
-fractional singular
integral on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We say that
T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}
, where
T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\equiv T^{\alpha}\left(f\sigma\right)
, is bounded
from
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
to
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)
if for all admissible truncations
\widetilde{T^{\alpha}}
we have

\left\|\widetilde{T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\leq\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }f\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right).

Here
\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
denotes the
best constant in these inequalities uniformly over all admissible
truncations of
T^{\alpha}
. See below for a precise definition of
admissible truncations, as well as the interpretation of the testing
conditions appearing in the next theorem. The case
s=0
of Theorem
2
is in
[
LaWi
]
and later in
[
SaShUr9
]
.

Finally, for
0\leq\alpha<n
we define the
\alpha
-fractional vector
Riesz transform kernel
\mathbf{K}^{\alpha}(x,y)
to be the standard
function
\mathbf{K}^{\alpha}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}
given by
c_{n,\alpha}\frac{z}{\left|z\right|^{n-\alpha}}
, and we denote by
\mathbf{R}^{\alpha}
the
associated
\alpha
-fractional singular integral on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Theorem 2

(
T1
for doubling measures)
.

Let
0\leq\alpha<n
, and let
\mathbf{R}^{\alpha}

be the
\alpha
-fractional vector Riesz transform on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Let
\sigma
and
\omega
be doubling Borel measures on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
there is a positive constant
\theta
, depending only on the doubling
constants of
\sigma
and
\omega
, such that if
0<s<\theta
, then
\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha}
, where
\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\equiv\mathbf{R}^{\alpha}\left(f\sigma\right)
, is bounded
from
W^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
to
W^{s}\left(\omega\right)
, i.e.

(1.3)

\left\|\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\leq\mathfrak{N}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha}}\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\sigma\right)},

provided the Sobolev
\mathbf{1}
-testing and
\mathbf{1}^{\ast}
-testing
conditions for the operator
T^{\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,s}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n},

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{R}_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W^{-s}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,-s,\ast}}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\ell\left(I\right)^{s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n},

taken over the family of indicator test functions
\left\{\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}
.

If we assume in addition that the classical pivotal constants
\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,1}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
and
\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,1,\ast}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)
hold, then for any smooth
\alpha
-fractional singular integral
T^{\alpha}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then
T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}
, where
T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\equiv T^{\alpha}\left(f\sigma\right)
, is bounded from
W^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
to
W^{s}\left(\omega\right)
, i.e.

(1.4)

\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\leq\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\sigma\right)},

provided the classical fractional Muckenhoupt condition on the measure pair
holds,

A_{2}^{\alpha}\equiv\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}\frac{\left|Q\right|_{\omega}\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|Q\right|^{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{n}\right)}}<\infty,

as well as the Sobolev
\mathbf{1}
-testing and
\mathbf{1}^{\ast}
-testing
conditions for the operator
T^{\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha,s}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n},

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W^{-s}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha,-s,\ast}}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\ell\left(I\right)^{s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n},

taken over the family of indicator test functions
\left\{\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}
.

Conversely, the testing conditions are necessary for (
1.3
),
and if in addition
T^{\alpha}
is a smooth convolution operator with
homogeneous kernel that is nonvanishing in some coordinate direction, then
A_{2}^{\alpha}<\infty
whenever the two weight norm inequality (
1.4
) holds for some
s>0
.

Remark 3
.

One can weaken the smoothness assumption on the kernel
K
depending on the
doubling constants of the measures
\sigma
and
\omega
, but we will not
pursue this here. See
[
Saw6
]
for sharper assumptions in the
L^{2}

case.

Problem 4
.

T1
theorems for Sobolev norms involving general measures, even for the
Hilbert transform on the line, remain open at this time.

The proof of Theorem
2
expands on that for
L^{2}

spaces with doubling measures using weighted Alpert wavelets (
[
AlSaUr
]
), but with a number of differences. For example:

(1)

The map
f\rightarrow\left|f\right|
fails to be bounded on

W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)
for
s>0
4
4

4

For example, if
d\mu=dx
and
f=\sum_{k=1}^{2N}\left(-1\right)^{k}1_{\left[k-1,k\right)}
, then
\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}}^{2}\approx N
, while
\left\|\left|f\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}}^{2}\approx N^{1+2s}
.
, which gives rise to
significant obstacles in dealing with bilinear inequalities (using the
L^{2}
inner product as a duality pairing) that require control of both
T:W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)\rightarrow W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)
and
T^{\ast}:W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)\rightarrow W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\sigma\right)
.

(2)

As a consequence, we are no longer able to use Calderón-Zygmund
decompositions and Carleson embedding theorems that require use of the
modulus
\left|f\right|
of a Sobolev function
f
. Instead, we
derive a new form of the pivotal condition, that permits a new Carleson
condition to circumvent these hurdles. These new conditions yield stronger
inequalities, but only over grids that are
full
.

(3)

The estimation of Sobolev norms in the paraproduct form requires the
use both of Alpert and Haar wavelets, in connection with the new Carleson
condition. This in turn requires the identification of different wavelet
spaces.

(4)

In Proposition
48
, we extend the Intertwining Proposition
from
[
Saw6
]
to Sobolev spaces using a new stronger form of the
\kappa
-pivotal condition, which also results in an overall simplification of this
proof.

(5)

A power decay of doubling measures near zero sets of polynomials is
needed to estimate Sobolev norms of moduli of Alpert wavelets in Lemma
27
when
s<0
, where the logarithmic decay obtained in
[
Saw6
]

is insufficient.

(6)

Finally, we prove the comparability of the various Sobolev space norms
for a fixed
s
and doubling measure (the case
s=0
being trivial),
including the familiar continuous norm in (
1.1
) when
s>0
. This
equivalence is needed in particular to implement the
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}/\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}

technology of Nazarov, Treil and Volberg.

Acknowledgement 5
.

We thank a referee for pointing out a gap in our treatment of the stopping
form in the original version of this paper, whose fix here requires

(1)

weakening the
T1
theorem to hold only for vector fractional Riesz
transforms
\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}
, and

(2)

weakening the two weight norm inequalities for general fractional
Calderón-Zygmund operators
T^{\alpha}
by requiring a pivotal side
condition.

2.
Preliminaries: Sobolev spaces and doubling measures

Denote by
\mathcal{Q}^{n}
the collection of cubes in
\mathbb{R}^{n}

having sides parallel to the coordinate axes. A positive locally finite
Borel measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is said to satisfy the
doubling condition
if there is a pair of constants
\left(\beta,\gamma\right)\in\left(0,1\right)^{2}
, called doubling parameters, such that

(2.1)

\left|\beta Q\right|_{\mu}\geq\gamma\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all cubes }Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n},

and the
reverse doubling condition
if there is a pair of constants
\left(\beta,\gamma\right)\in\left(0,1\right)^{2}
, called reverse
doubling parameters, such that

(2.2)

\left|\beta Q\right|_{\mu}\leq\gamma\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all cubes }Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}.

Note that the inequality in (
2.2
) has been reversed from
that in the definition of the doubling condition in (
2.1
). A
familiar equivalent reformulation of (
2.1
) is that there is a
positive constant
C_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
, called the doubling constant, such
that
\left|2Q\right|_{\mu}\leq C_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}
for all cubes
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. There is also a
positive constant
\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
, called a
doubling exponent
, such that

\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}\frac{\left|sQ\right|_{\mu}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\leq s^{\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all sufficiently large }s>0.

It is well known (see e.g. the introduction in
[
SaUr
]
) that doubling
implies reverse doubling, and that
\mu
is reverse doubling if and only if
there exists a positive constant
\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}
, called a

reverse doubling exponent
, such that

\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}\frac{\left|sQ\right|_{\mu}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\leq s^{\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all sufficiently small }s>0.

In particular, for
I\subset Q
, we have

(2.3)

\frac{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}=\frac{\left|\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}I\right|_{\mu}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}I\right|_{\mu}}\leq\frac{\left|3Q\right|_{\mu}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(Q\right)}\right)^{\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\leq C_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(Q\right)}\right)^{\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}.

2.1.
Decay of doubling measures near zero sets of polynomials

In order to deal with Sobolev norms and doubling measures, we will need the
following estimate on doubling measures of ‘halos’ of zero sets of
normalized polynomials, which follows the same plan of proof as in the case
of boundaries of cubes proved in
[
Saw6
, Lemma 24]
. We first recall a
slight variant of a remark from
[
Saw6
]
.

For any polynomial
P
and cube
Q
, we say that
P
is
Q
-normalized

if
\left\|P\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(Q\right)}=1
.

Remark 6
.

Since all norms on a finite dimensional vector space are
equivalent, we have upon rescaling the cube
Q
to the unit cube,

(2.4)

\left\|P\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(Q\right)}\approx\left|P\left(0\right)\right|+\sqrt{n}\ell\left(Q\right)\left\|\nabla P\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(Q\right)},\ \ \ \ \ \deg P<\kappa,

with implicit constants depending only on
n
and
\kappa
. In particular
there is a positive constant
K_{n,\kappa}
such that
\sqrt{n}\ell\left(Q\right)\left\|\nabla P\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(Q\right)}\leq K_{n,\kappa}
for all
Q
-normalized polynomials
P
. Then for every
Q
-normalized polynomial
P
of degree less than
\kappa
, there is a ball
B\left(y,\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2K_{n,\kappa}}\ell\left(Q\right)\right)\subset Q\
on which
P
is nonvanishing. Indeed, if there is no such ball,
then

1=\left\|P\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(Q\right)}\leq\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2K_{n,\kappa}}\ell\left(Q\right)\left\|\nabla P\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(Q\right)}\leq\frac{1}{2}

is a contradiction.

Here is the result proved in
[
Saw6
, Lemma 24]
.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose
\mu
is a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. Then for
0<\delta<1
we have

\left|Q\setminus\left(1-\delta\right)Q\right|_{\mu}\leq\frac{C}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\ .

We will need to improve significantly on this as follows. Without loss of
generality, suppose that
Q=\left[0,1\right]\times\left[0,1\right]
in
the plane and
d\mu\left(x,y\right)=w\left(x,y\right)dxdy
. Define
W

to be even on
\left[-1,1\right]
by

W\left(y\right)\equiv\int_{0}^{1}w\left(x,y\right)dx,\ \ \ \ \ 0\leq y\leq 1.

and note that
W\left(y\right)dy
is a doubling measure on
\left[0,1\right]
, hence also reverse doubling with exponent
\theta^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}
.
Thus from the reverse doubling property applied to the subinterval
\left[0,t\right]
of
\left[-1,1\right]
we have that

\int_{0}^{t}W\left(y\right)dy\leq Ct^{\theta^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\int_{-1}^{1}W\left(y\right)dy\leq C^{\prime}t^{\theta^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\int_{0}^{1}W\left(y\right)dy,

which says that

(2.5)

\left|\left[0,1\right]\times\left[0,t\right]\right|_{\mu}=\int_{0}^{t}W\left(y\right)dy\leq C^{\prime}t^{\theta^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\int_{0}^{1}W\left(y\right)dy=C^{\prime}t^{\theta^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\left|\left[0,1\right]\times\left[0,1\right]\right|_{\mu}.

This gives power decay instead of logarithmic decay, which will prove
crucial below. The next lemma is a generalization of
[
Saw6
, Lemma 24]
.

Lemma 8
.

Let
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose
\mu
is a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. Let
Z
denote the zero set
of a
Q
-normalized polynomial
P
of degree less than
\kappa
, and for
0<\delta<1
, let

Z_{\delta}=\left\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\left|y-z\right|<\delta\text{ for some }z\in Z\right\}

denote the
\delta
-halo of
Z
. Then for a positive constant
C_{n,\kappa}
depending only on
n
and
\kappa
, and not on
P
itself, we have

\left|Q\cap Z_{\delta}\right|_{\mu}\leq\frac{C_{n,\kappa}}{\ln\frac{1}{\delta}}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\ .

Proof.

Let
\delta=2^{-m}
. Denote by
\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right)
the set of
m^{th}
generation dyadic children of
Q
, so that
each
I\in\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right)
has side length
\ell\left(I\right)=2^{-m}\ell\left(Q\right)
, and define the
collections

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{I\in\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right):I\subset Q\text{ and }\partial I\cap Z\neq\emptyset\right\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{I\in\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right):3I\subset Q\text{ and }\partial\left(3I\right)\cap Z\neq\emptyset\right\}.

Then

Q\cap Z_{\delta}=\bigcup_{I\in\mathfrak{G}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right)}I\text{ and }Q\setminus Z_{\delta}=\overset{\cdot}{\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}}_{k=2}^{m}\bigcup_{I\in\mathfrak{H}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)}I.

From Remark
6
, we obtain that the the union
\bigcup_{I\in\mathfrak{H}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)}RI
contains
Q\cap Z_{\delta}
for
k\geq cm
for some
c=c_{n,\kappa}\in\left(0,1\right)

depending only on
n
and
\kappa
, and in particular independent of
m
.
Then from the doubling condition we have
\left|RI\right|_{\mu}\leq D_{R}\left|I\right|_{\mu}
for all cubes
I
and some
constant
D_{R}
, and so for
k\geq cm
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathfrak{H}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)\right|_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathfrak{H}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)}\left|I\right|_{\mu}\geq\sum_{I\in\mathfrak{H}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)}\frac{1}{D_{R}}\left|RI\right|_{\mu}=\frac{1}{D_{R}}\int\left(\sum_{I\in\mathfrak{H}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)}\mathbf{1}_{RI}\right)d\mu

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{D_{R}}\int\left(\sum_{I\in\mathfrak{G}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right)d\mu=\frac{1}{D_{R}}\left|\mathfrak{G}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)\right|_{\mu}\geq\frac{1}{D_{R}}\left|\mathfrak{G}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right)\right|_{\mu}=\frac{1}{D_{R}}\left|Q\cap Z_{\delta}\right|_{\mu}\ .

Thus we have

\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\geq\sum_{k=cm}^{m}\left|\mathfrak{H}^{\left(k\right)}\left(Q\right)\right|_{\mu}\geq\frac{m\left(1-c\right)}{D_{R}}\left|Q\cap Z_{\delta}\right|_{\mu}\ ,

which proves the lemma.

We can apply the method used in (
2.5
) to obtain a power decay
instead of a logarithmic decay.

Corollary 9
.

Let
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose
\mu
is a
doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}
. Let
Z

denote the zero set of a
Q
-normalized polynomial
P
of degree less than
\kappa
, and for
0<\delta<1
, let
Z_{\delta}
denote the
\delta
-halo
of
Z
. Then for a positive constant
C_{n,\kappa}
depending only on
n

and
\kappa
, and not on
P
itself, and for some
\theta>0
, we have

\left|Q\cap Z_{\delta}\right|_{\mu}\leq C_{n,\kappa}\delta^{\theta}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\ .

In particular this holds for
Z=\partial Q
, which is a finite union of zero
sets of linear functions.

Proof.

Without loss of generality
Q
is the unit cube
\left[0,1\right]^{n}
.
Define an even function
w\left(t\right)
on
\left[-1,1\right]
, that
is increasing on
\left[0,1\right]
, by the formula

w\left(t\right)\equiv\left|Z_{t}\right|_{\mu}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ 0\leq t\leq 1.

Since
P
is a
Q
-normalized polynomial of degree less than
\kappa
,
there are positive constants
t_{0},\,c_{0},C_{0},A
such that for every
0<t<t_{0}
, there is a collection of cubes
\left\{Q_{i}^{t}\right\}_{i}

with

\displaystyle\ell\left(Q_{i}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{0}t,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{Z_{t}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\mathbf{1}_{Q_{i}^{t}}\left(x\right)\leq A\mathbf{1}_{Z_{t}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{Z_{2t}}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i}\mathbf{1}_{C_{0}Q_{i}^{t}}\left(x\right)\leq A.

Thus we have

w\left(2t\right)\leq\sum_{i}\left|C_{0}Q_{i}^{t}\right|_{\mu}\leq\sum_{i}C_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}\left|Q_{i}^{t}\right|_{\mu}\leq C_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}M\left|Z_{t}\right|_{\mu}=C_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}Aw\left(t\right),\ \ \ \ \ 0<t<t_{0},

and hence there is a doubling exponent
\theta_{w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
such
that

\frac{w\left(st\right)}{w\left(t\right)}\leq s^{\theta_{w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all sufficiently large }s.

We claim
w\left(t\right)
also satisfies the reverse doubling condition

w\left(\delta\right)\leq C\delta^{\varepsilon}w\left(1\right),\ \ \ \ \ 0<\delta<t_{0}.

Indeed, let
d\mu\equiv\frac{dw}{dt}
. Then assuming
s\geq 5
in the
definition of
\theta_{w}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
, we obtain for
Q=\left[0,t\right]
that

\displaystyle\left|3Q\setminus Q\right|_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset 3Q\setminus Q,\ell\left(I\right)=\ell\left(Q\right)}\left|I\right|_{\mu}\geq\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset 3Q\setminus Q,\ell\left(I\right)=\ell\left(Q\right)}5^{-\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}}\left|5I\right|_{\mu}\geq\left(3^{n}-1\right)5^{-\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\left|Q\right|_{\mu}=\left|3Q\right|_{\mu}-\left|3Q\setminus Q\right|_{\mu}\leq\left(1-\frac{3^{n}-1}{5^{\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}}}\right)\left|3Q\right|_{\mu}\ ,

which gives reverse doubling, and hence

\left|Q\cap Z_{\delta}\right|_{\mu}=\int_{0}^{\delta}w\left(t\right)dt\leq C\delta^{\varepsilon}\left|Q\right|_{\mu},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }0<\delta<t_{0},

and trivially this is extended to
\delta<1
by possibly increasing the
constant
C
.

2.2.
Weighted Alpert bases for
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
and

L^{\infty}
control of projections

The following theorem was proved in
[
RaSaWi
]
, which establishes the
existence of Alpert wavelets, for
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
in all
dimensions, having the three important properties of orthogonality,
telescoping and moment vanishing. Since the statement is simplified for
doubling measures, and this is the only case considered in our main theorem,
we restrict ourselves to this case here.

We first recall the basic construction of weighted Alpert wavelets in
[
RaSaWi
]
restricted to doubling measures. Let
\mu
be a doubling measure
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and fix
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
. For
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}
, the collection of cubes with sides parallel to the coordinate axes,
denote by
L_{Q;\kappa}^{2}\left(\mu\right)
the finite dimensional
subspace of
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
that consists of linear combinations
of the indicators of the children
\mathfrak{C}\left(Q\right)
of
Q

multiplied by polynomials of degree less than
\kappa
, and such that the
linear combinations have vanishing
\mu
-moments on the cube
Q
up to
order
\kappa-1
:

L_{Q;\kappa}^{2}\left(\mu\right)\equiv\left\{f=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{Q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}\left(Q\right)}\mathbf{1}_{Q^{\prime}}p_{Q^{\prime};\kappa}\left(x\right):\int_{Q}f\left(x\right)x^{\beta}d\mu\left(x\right)=0,\ \ \ \text{for }0\leq\left|\beta\right|<\kappa\right\},

where
p_{Q^{\prime};\kappa}\left(x\right)=\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{n}:\left|\beta\right|\leq\kappa-1\ }a_{Q^{\prime};\beta}x^{\beta}
is a polynomial in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
of degree less than
\kappa
. Here
x^{\beta}=x_{1}^{\beta_{1}}x_{2}^{\beta_{2}}...x_{n}^{\beta_{n}}
. Let
d_{Q;\kappa}\equiv\dim L_{Q;\kappa}^{2}\left(\mu\right)

be the dimension of the finite dimensional linear space
L_{Q;\kappa}^{2}\left(\mu\right)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
denote a dyadic grid on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and for
Q\in\mathcal{D}
, let
\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}
denote orthogonal
projection onto the finite dimensional subspace
L_{Q;\kappa}^{2}\left(\mu\right)
, and let
\mathbb{E}_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}
denote orthogonal
projection onto the finite dimensional subspace

\mathcal{P}_{Q;\kappa}^{n}\left(\sigma\right)\equiv\mathnormal{\mathop{\mathrm{S}pan}}\{\mathbf{1}_{Q}x^{\beta}:0\leq\left|\beta\right|<\kappa\}.

Theorem 10

(Weighted Alpert Bases)
.

Let
\mu
be a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, fix
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, and fix a dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

(1)

Then
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}
is a complete set of orthogonal projections in
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
and

(2.6)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f,\ \ \ \ \ f\in L^{2}\left(\mu\right),

\displaystyle\left\langle\bigtriangleup_{P;\kappa}^{\mu}f,\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}=0\text{ for }P\neq Q,

where convergence in the first line holds both in
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)

norm and pointwise
\mu
-almost everywhere.

(2)

Moreover we have the telescoping identities

(2.7)

\mathbf{1}_{Q}\sum_{I:\ Q\subsetneqq I\subset P}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}=\mathbb{E}_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}-\mathbf{1}_{Q}\mathbb{E}_{P;\kappa}^{\mu}\ \text{ \ for }P,Q\in\mathcal{D}\text{ with }Q\subsetneqq P,

(3)

and the moment vanishing conditions

(2.8)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)\ x^{\beta}d\mu\left(x\right)=0,\ \ \ \text{for }Q\in\mathcal{D},\text{ }\beta\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{n},\ 0\leq\left|\beta\right|<\kappa\ .

We can fix an orthonormal basis
\left\{h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\}_{a\in\Gamma_{Q,n,\kappa}}
of
L_{Q;\kappa}^{2}\left(\mu\right)

where
\Gamma_{Q,n,\kappa}
is a convenient finite index set. Then

\left\{h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\}_{a\in\Gamma_{Q,n,\kappa}\text{
and }Q\in\mathcal{D}}

is an orthonormal basis for
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
. In particular we
have

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\widehat{f}\left(Q\right)\right|^{2},

\displaystyle\text{where }\widehat{f}\left(Q\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\left\langle f,h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{\mu}\right\}_{a\in\Gamma_{Q,n,\kappa}}\text{ and }\left|\widehat{f}\left(Q\right)\right|^{2}\equiv\sum_{a\in\Gamma_{Q,n,\kappa}\text{ }}\left|\left\langle f,h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{\mu}\right|^{2}.

In terms of the Alpert coefficient vectors
\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\equiv\left\{\left\langle f,a_{I;\kappa,j}^{\mu}\right\rangle\right\}_{j=1}^{\kappa}
, we have for the special case of a doubling
measure
\mu
(see
[
Saw6
, (4.7) on page 14]
),

(2.9)

\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|=\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\leq\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mu\right)}\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}\leq C\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}=C\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|,

and in particular,

(2.10)

\left\|h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mu\right)}\approx\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}},

since
\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}=h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}
.

Notation 11
.

For doubling measures
\mu
, the cardinality of
\Gamma_{Q,n,\kappa}

depends only on
n
and
\kappa
, which are usually known from context, and
so we will simply write
\Gamma
when
\mu
is doubling.

From now on, all measures considered will be assumed to be doubling, and
often without explicit mention. We now inroduce Sobolev spaces defined by
weighted Alpert projections instead of weighted Haar projections. We show
below that these spaces are actually equivalent. For convenience we repeat
the definition of weighted Sobolev space used in this paper.

Definition 12
.

Given
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathcal{D}\in\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}
a dyadic grid, we define the
\kappa
-dyadic
homogeneous
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
-Sobolev norm of
a function
f\in L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
by

\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\equiv\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\ ,

and we denote by
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
the
corresponding Hilbert space completion.

Lemma 13
.

The set
\left\{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\}_{\left(Q,a\right)\in\mathcal{D}\times\Gamma}
is an
orthonormal basis for
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
, and
thus for any subset
\mathcal{H}
of the dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
, we have,

(2.11)

\left\|\sum_{\left(I,a\right)\in\mathcal{H}\times\Gamma}c_{I,a}\ell\left(I\right)^{s}h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{\left(I,a\right)\in\mathcal{H}\times\Gamma}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left\langle\ell\left(I\right)^{s}h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a},\ell\left(J\right)^{s}h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,b}\right\rangle_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\langle\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\ell\left(I\right)^{s}h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a},\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\ell\left(J\right)^{s}h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,b}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\ell\left(I\right)^{s}\ell\left(J\right)^{s}\left\langle\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a},\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,b}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&\text{ if }&I=J\text{ and }a=b\\
0&\text{ if }&I\not=J\text{ or }a\not=b\end{array}\right.,

since
\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}h_{K;\kappa}^{\mu,a}
vanishes if

Q\not=K
, and equals
h_{K;\kappa}^{\mu,a}
if
Q=K
. Thus
\left\{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\}_{\left(Q,a\right)\in\mathcal{D}\times\Gamma}
is an orthonormal basis and we conclude that (
2.11
) holds.

2.3.
Equivalence of Sobolev spaces

In
[
Tri
]
, Triebel defines the usual homogeneous unweighted Sobolev
space
W^{s}
with norm
\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}}
given by

\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}}^{2}\equiv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\left(-\bigtriangleup\right)^{\frac{s}{2}}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\left(\left|\xi\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{s}{2}}\widehat{f}\left(\xi\right)\right|^{2}d\xi,

and the corresponding inhomogeneous version with norm squared
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\left(I-\bigtriangleup\right)^{\frac{s}{2}}f\left(x\right)\right|^{2}dx
, which we will not consider here. Combining
results of Triebel
[
Tri
]
with those of Seeger and Ullrich
[
SeUl
]

shows that

Lemma 14

(
[
Tri
]
,
[
SeUl
]
)
.

The following three statements are equivalent for
\ \mu
equal to Lebesgue
measure and
s\in\mathbb{R}
.

(1)

W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}=W^{s}
,

(2)

\left\{\ell\left(I\right)^{s}h_{I}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{D}}

is an orthonormal basis for
W^{s}
,

(3)

-\frac{1}{2}<s<\frac{1}{2}
.

Here is the first step toward proving the equivalence of the different
dyadic Sobolev spaces over all grids
\mathcal{D}
and integers
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, which in particular is used to implement the
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}/\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}
cube technology of Nazarov, Treil and Volberg. The reader can notice
that the doubling property of the measure
\mu
is not explicitly used in
this argument, rather only in the definition of the Sobolev spaces.

Lemma 15
.

Let
\mu
be a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and let
\mathcal{D}
be a dyadic grid on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then for
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have,

W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right),

with equivalence of norms.

Proof.

We first claim that for
s<0
,

(2.13)

W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)\subset W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)\text{ for }1\leq\kappa_{1}\leq\kappa_{2},

which by duality gives for
s>0
,

(2.14)

W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)\subset W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)\text{ for }s>0\text{ and }1\leq\kappa_{1}\leq\kappa_{2}.

Indeed, for any subset
\mathcal{H}\subset\mathcal{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}:\ Q\subset I}c_{I,a}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I,I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa},a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ Q\subset I\cap I^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}c_{I,a}\left(\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right)c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\left(\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}\right)d\mu

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle A+B,

where the first term satisfies

\displaystyle A=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\left\|h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\leq C\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\left\|h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}=C\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

and where the final equality follows from Lemma
13
.

To handle the second term, we write

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\neq I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa},a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\{\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\subset I\cap I^{\prime}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa},a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}}+\sum_{I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I\in\mathcal{H};\ a,a^{\prime}\in\Gamma}\right\}c_{I,a}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\{\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\subset I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}d\mu

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle B_{1}+B_{2}\ ,

where
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
are symmetric. So it suffices to estimate

B_{1}=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\{\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\subset I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\}\left\{\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}\right\}d\mu,

where

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\subset I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\subset I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-4s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}.

So for
s<0
we have the estimate,

\displaystyle B_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I,a}\right|\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left(\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}}\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left(\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\right)},

where the second factor squared is

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left(\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I\subsetneqq I^{\prime}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}

since
s<0
. Altogether we have

B_{1}\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}=C\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

which together with the estimate for term
I
proves our claim (
2.13
).

Now we claim that for
s>0
we have

(2.15)

W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)\subset W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)\text{ for all }\kappa_{1}\geq\kappa_{2}\geq 1.

Indeed, for any subset
\mathcal{H}\subset\mathcal{D}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}:\ Q\subset I}c_{I,a}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I,I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa},a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ Q\supset I\vee I^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}c_{I,a}\left(\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right)c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\left(\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}\right)d\mu

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle A+B,

where
I\vee I^{\prime}
denotes the smallest dyadic cube containing both
I
and
I^{\prime}
if it exists; otherwise the sum over
Q\supset I\vee I^{\prime}
is empty. Just as before, the first term satisfies

\displaystyle A=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\left\|h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\leq C\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\left\|h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left(c_{I,a}\right)^{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}=C\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

and where the final equality follows from Lemma
13
.

To handle the second term
B
, we only need to consider the two cases
I^{\prime}\subset I
and
I^{\prime}\cap I=\emptyset,\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)
. For the first case, we have
the estimate,

\displaystyle B_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ase}1}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\{\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\supset I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\}\left\{\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}\right\}d\mu\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I,a}\right|\left\|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\supset I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\left\|\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)},

where for
s>0
,

\left\|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\supset I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\leq\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\supset I}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s},

and

\left\|\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}=\sqrt{\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}}.

Thus we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle B_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ase}1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I,a}\right|\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\sqrt{\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}}\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}}

where

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}:\ I^{\prime}\subsetneqq I}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}.

Altogether we have

B_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ase}1}\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\approx\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

which is the desired estimate for
B_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ase}1}
.

Turning finally to the second case
I^{\prime}\cap I=\emptyset,\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)
, we have

\displaystyle B_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ase}2}\equiv\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa},a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ Q\supset I\vee I^{\prime}\\
I^{\prime}\cap I=\emptyset,\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}c_{I,a}\left(\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right)c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\left(\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}\right)d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ K\subset Q}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ \pi^{\left(m\right)}\left(I^{\prime}\right)\subset K\\
\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}c_{I,a}\left(\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right)c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\left(\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}\right)d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ K\subset Q}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ \pi^{\left(m\right)}\left(I^{\prime}\right)\subset K\\
\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a^{\prime}}\right)d\mu.

Now we compute that for each
K\in\mathcal{D}
,

\left\|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ \pi^{\left(m\right)}\left(I^{\prime}\right)\subset K\\
\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ \pi^{\left(m\right)}\left(I^{\prime}\right)\subset K\\
\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ K\subset Q}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\left[\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ K\subset Q}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-4s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\left[\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ K\subset Q}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-4s}\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\ell\left(K\right)^{-4s}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}=C2^{-4sm}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-4s}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2},

and hence

\displaystyle B_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ase}2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ K\subset Q}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ \pi^{\left(m\right)}\left(I^{\prime}\right)\subset K\\
\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}h_{I^{\prime};\kappa_{2}}^{\mu,a}\right)d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}C2^{-sm}\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}}\sqrt{\ell\left(K\right)^{-2s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ \pi^{\left(m\right)}\left(I^{\prime}\right)\subset K\\
\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}C2^{-sm}\sqrt{\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H}\cap\mathfrak{C}^{\left(m\right)}\left(K\right);\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(K\right)^{-2s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}:\ \pi^{\left(m\right)}\left(I^{\prime}\right)\subset K\\
\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}},

and regrouping we obtain

\displaystyle B_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ase}2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}C2^{-sm}\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}\sum_{K\in\mathcal{D}:\ \pi^{\left(m\right)}\left(I^{\prime}\right)\subset K}\ell\left(K\right)^{-2s}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}C2^{-2sm}\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H};\ a^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{I^{\prime},n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|c_{I^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\right|^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|c_{I,a}\right|^{2}\approx\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{H};\ a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa}}c_{I,a}h_{I;\kappa_{2}}^{\mu}\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

which is the desired estimate for
B_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ase}2}
.

Thus from (
2.14
) and (
2.13
) we obtain
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{1}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa_{2}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
for all
s>0
, all grids
\mathcal{D}
and all integers
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
. Duality now establishes these
equalities for
s<0
as well, and the case
s=0
is automatic.

Following Peetre
[
Pee
]
and Stein
[
Ste
]
, we define the homogeneous

difference
Sobolev space
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
by

W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)\equiv\left\{f\in L^{2}\left(\mu\right):\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};\kappa}^{s}}\left(\mu\right)<\infty\right\},\ \ \ \ \ s\in\mathbb{R}\text{ and }\kappa\in\mathbb{N},

where

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{Q}\left|\frac{f\left(x\right)-\mathbb{E}_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}}\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x\right),

\displaystyle\text{and }\mathbb{E}_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(E_{Q}^{\mu}f\right)\mathbf{1}_{Q}\left(x\right)=\left(\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\int_{Q}fd\mu\right)\mathbf{1}_{Q}\left(x\right).

The proof of the next lemma does not explicitly use the doubling property of

\mu
either.

Lemma 16
.

Suppose
\mu
is a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathcal{D}

is a dyadic grid on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then for
s>0
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right),

with equivalence of norms.

Proof.

We expand the function

f\left(x\right)-\mathbb{E}_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)=\sum_{I\subset Q}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\left(x\right),

and so obtain for
s>0
that

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};\kappa}^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\sum_{I\subset Q}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\in\mathcal{D}\\
Q\supset I\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{-2s}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\ .

We just showed in Lemma
15
above that the weighted Alpert Sobolev
spaces
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
coincide for
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\kappa\geq 1
, and so we simply write
W_{\mathcal{D}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
for these spaces, and for specificity we use the
norm of
W_{\mathcal{D};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
. Now we will show that
the spaces
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
are independent
of the dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}
for
\left|s\right|
sufficiently
small provided the measure
\mu
is
doubling
, and extend this to
include the difference spaces
W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
as well.

Theorem 17
.

Suppose
\mu
is a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}

and
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{E}
are dyadic grids on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Then for
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\left|s\right|

sufficiently small, we have

W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{E};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right).

Proof.

Note that for
J\in\mathcal{E}
and
0<\delta=2^{-m}\leq 1
, and
F
is
any finite linear combination of Alpert wavelets,

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)=\delta\ell\left(J\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)=\delta\ell\left(J\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\left(\mathbf{1}_{H_{\delta}\left(J\right)}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right)\right|^{2}d\mu\leq\int_{H_{\delta}\left(J\right)}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{H_{\delta}\left(J\right)}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right\|_{\infty}^{2}d\mu=\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left|H_{\delta}\left(J\right)\right|_{\mu}\leq\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right\|_{\infty}^{2}C\delta^{\varepsilon}\left|J\right|_{\mu}\leq C\delta^{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu,

which gives for
\eta>0

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)}\ell\left(I\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)=2^{-m}\ell\left(J\right)}\ell\left(I\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}C2^{m\left(2s+\eta-\varepsilon\right)}\ell\left(J\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu\leq C_{\varepsilon,\eta,s}\ell\left(J\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu,

provided
s<\frac{\varepsilon-\eta}{2}
.

On the other hand, for
J\in\mathcal{E}
and
\delta=2^{m}>1
, there are
at most
2^{n}
cubes
I
such that
\ell\left(I\right)=\delta\ell\left(J\right)
and
I\cap J\neq\emptyset
, and then following the line
of reasoning in (
2.16
) we have,

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)>\ell\left(J\right)}\ell\left(I\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)>\ell\left(J\right)\text{ and }I\cap J\neq\emptyset}\ell\left(I\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)>\ell\left(J\right)\text{ and }I\cap J\neq\emptyset}\ell\left(I\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\left|I\right|_{\mu}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\approx\left|J\right|_{\mu}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)>\ell\left(J\right)\text{ and }I\cap J\neq\emptyset}\ell\left(I\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\frac{\left|J\right|_{\mu}}{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|J\right|_{\mu}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right\|_{\infty}^{2}C_{\eta}\ell\left(J\right)^{\eta}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{-m\left(2s+\eta\right)}\ell\left(J\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\frac{\left|J\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\pi^{n}J\right|_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2}d\mu\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{-m\left(2s+\eta\right)}c2^{-m\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\leq C\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}F\right|^{2},

provided
s>\frac{\eta-\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}{2}
where
\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}>0
is the reverse doubling exponent of
\mu
, i.e.

\left|\pi^{n}J\right|_{\mu}\geq c2^{n\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\left|J\right|_{\mu}.

Now we compute

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\left(\sum_{J\in\mathcal{E}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}\right)f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\sum_{J\in\mathcal{E}:\ \ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\sum_{J\in\mathcal{E}:\ \ell\left(I\right)>\ell\left(J\right)}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}.

We bound the first sum by

\displaystyle C_{\eta}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{E}:\ \ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)}\left(\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{\eta}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\eta}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{E}}\ell\left(J\right)^{\eta}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ \ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)}\ell\left(I\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\eta}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{E}}\ell\left(J\right)^{\eta}C_{\varepsilon,\eta,s}\ell\left(J\right)^{-\left(2s+\eta\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right|^{2}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon,\eta,s}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{E}}\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right|^{2}d\mu=C_{\varepsilon,\eta,s}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{E};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

and the second sum by

\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{E}:\ \ell\left(I\right)>\ell\left(J\right)}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}\right)^{\eta}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\leq C_{\eta}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{E};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

provided
s>\frac{\eta-\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}{2}
. Altogether we
obtain

\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}\leq C_{\eta}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{E};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}\ ,

provided

\frac{\eta-\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}{2}<s<\frac{\varepsilon-\eta}{2},

and interchanging the roles of the dyadic grids
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{E}
completes the proof.

Finally, we will explicitly compute the norm
\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}
by starting with

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\int_{Q}\int_{Q}\left(f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)\right)^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\int_{Q}\int_{Q}\left\{f\left(x\right)^{2}-2f\left(x\right)f\left(y\right)+f\left(y\right)^{2}\right\}d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int_{Q}f^{2}d\mu-2\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\left(E_{Q}^{\mu}f\right)^{2}=2\int_{Q}\left\{f\left(x\right)^{2}-\left(E_{Q}^{\mu}f\right)^{2}\right\}d\mu,

to obtain the representation

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{Q}\left|\frac{f\left(x\right)-E_{Q}^{\mu}f}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}}\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\int_{Q}\left\{f\left(x\right)^{2}-2\left(E_{Q}^{\mu}f\right)f\left(x\right)+\left(E_{Q}^{\mu}f\right)^{2}\right\}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\int_{Q}\left\{f\left(x\right)^{2}-\left(E_{Q}^{\mu}f\right)^{2}\right\}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\int_{Q}\int_{Q}\left(f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)\right)^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right).

We will next show that this last expression is comparable to the expression

\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\equiv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)}{\left|x-y\right|^{s}}\right)^{2}\frac{d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)}{\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}}.

Theorem 18
.

Suppose that
\mu
is doubling on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For
s>0
sufficiently small, we have

\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\approx\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}.

Proof.

From the formula above we have

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\{\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{Q\times Q}\left(x,y\right)}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\right\}\left(f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)\right)^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{C}{\left|x-y\right|^{2s}\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}}\left(f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)\right)^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)=\frac{C}{2}\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

since
\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\gtrsim\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}
whenever
\left(x,y\right)\in Q\times Q
. Conversely we use the one third trick for
dyadic grids. Namely that there is a finite collection of dyadic grids
\left\{\mathcal{D}_{m}\right\}_{m=1}^{3^{n}}
so that for every
\left(x,y\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
, there is some
m
and
some
Q\in\mathcal{D}_{m}
such that

B\left(x,C\left|x-y\right|\right),B\left(y,C\left|x-y\right|\right)\subset Q\text{ and }\ell\left(Q\right)\leq C\left|x-y\right|,

where
C
is a large constant that will be fixed below. In particular this
gives

\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\approx\left|B\left(x,c\left|x-y\right|\right)\right|_{\mu}\approx\left|B\left(y,c\left|x-y\right|\right)\right|_{\mu},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for
}\frac{1}{100}<c<\frac{1}{2}.

Then we cover the product space
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
with a
collection of product balls

\left\{B\left(x_{k},c\left|x_{k}-y_{k}\right|\right)\times B\left(y_{k},c\left|x_{k}-y_{k}\right|\right)\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}

where
E\equiv\left\{\left(x_{k},y_{k}\right)\right\}_{k=1}^{\infty}

is a discrete subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and provided
c
is chosen sufficiently small, this collection of product balls has
bounded overlap. Now denote by
Q_{k}
the cube chosen above by the point
\left(x_{k},y_{k}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then we
have

\displaystyle\left|Q_{k}\right|_{\mu}\approx\left|B\left(x,c\left|x-y\right|\right)\right|_{\mu}\approx\left|B\left(y,c\left|x-y\right|\right)\right|_{\mu}

\displaystyle\text{for all }\left(x,y\right)\text{ in the product ball }B\left(x_{k},c\left|x_{k}-y_{k}\right|\right)\times B\left(y_{k},c\left|x_{k}-y_{k}\right|\right),

and so

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{B\left(x_{k},c\left|x_{k}-y_{k}\right|\right)}\int_{B\left(y_{k},c\left|x_{k}-y_{k}\right|\right)}\left(\frac{f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)}{\left|x-y\right|^{s}}\right)^{2}\frac{d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)}{\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{Q_{k}}\int_{Q_{k}}\left(\frac{f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)}{\ell\left(Q_{k}\right)^{s}}\right)^{2}\frac{d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)}{\left|Q_{k}\right|_{\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{3^{n}}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}_{m}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\int_{Q}\int_{Q}\left(f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)\right)^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{m=1}^{3^{n}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{m,\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\leq C\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

since
\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}_{m,\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}
is independent of the dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}_{m}
by
Theorem
17
.

In particular, we have thus obtained one of the main results of this
subsection.

Theorem 19
.

For all grids
\mathcal{D}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\,
, all
positive integers
\kappa
, and all sufficiently small
s>0
depending only on the doubling constant of
\mu
, we have

W^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right),

with equivalence of norms.

As a consequence of this theorem, there is essentially just one notion of a
weighted Sobolev space for a doubling measure
\mu
provided
\left|s\right|
is sufficiently small, namely
W^{s}\left(\mu\right)
when

s>0
, and any of the dyadic spaces
W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
when
s<0
. For specificity we will use the norm of
W_{\mathcal{D}_{0};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
on these spaces, where
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is
the standard dyadic grid on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Definition 20
.

Define
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{D};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W_{\mathcal{D}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff}};\kappa}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
for
\left|s\right|
sufficiently small, and norm
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
with the norm of
W_{\mathcal{D};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
.

Note that
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)=W^{s}\left(\mu\right)
for
s>0
sufficiently small.

Remark 21
.

The Sobolev space
W^{s}\left(\mu\right)
used here is different from the
Sobolev space introduced on a space of homogeneous type in
[
HaSa
]
,
since one can show that the norm squared used in
[
HaSa
]
is comparable to

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)}{\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}^{\alpha}}\right)^{2}\frac{d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)}{\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}}.

It seems likely that our proof extends to the analogous
T1
theorem for
these weighted Sobolev spaces using the doubling measure inequalities
\frac{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}{\left|2^{m}Q\right|_{\mu}}\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)}{\ell\left(2^{m}Q\right)}\right)^{\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}
and
\frac{\left|2^{m}Q\right|_{\mu}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell\left(2^{m}Q\right)}{\ell\left(Q\right)}\right)^{\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}}
.

Problem 22
.

Does a
T1
theorem hold in the context of weighted Sobolev spaces with
doubling measures and norm squared given by

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)}{\varphi\left(\left|x-y\right|\right)}\right)^{2}\frac{d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)}{\left|B\left(\frac{x+y}{2},\frac{\left|x-y\right|}{2}\right)\right|_{\mu}},

where
\varphi:\left(0,\infty\right)\rightarrow\left(0,\infty\right)

satisfies

\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{\theta_{1}}\lesssim\frac{\varphi\left(s\right)}{\varphi\left(t\right)}\lesssim\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{\theta_{2}}\text{ for }0<s\leq t<\infty\ ?

Remark 23
.

The inner product for the Hilbert space
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
is given by

\left\langle f,g\right\rangle_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}\equiv\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\langle\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f,\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}g\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}

where the inner product for
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
is given by

\left\langle f,g\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\equiv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f\left(x\right)g\left(x\right)d\mu\left(x\right).

In Lemma
13
we showed that
\left\{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}
is a
