complete set of orthogonal projections on
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
, nevertheless we will not use the Hilbert space duality that
identifies the dual of a Hilbert space with the conjugate of itself under
the inner product
\left\langle f,g\right\rangle_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}
, but rather the
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
inner product
which identifies the dual of
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
with
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)
. As mentioned in the introduction,
the reason for this is that the weighted Alpert projections
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}
satisfy
telescoping identities, while the orthogonal projections
\left\{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}
do not.

2.4.
Haar, Alpert and indicator functions

The Alpert projections
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{D}}
form a complete family of orthogonal projections on
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
, where

\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\equiv\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f-\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}\left(I\right)}\mathbb{E}_{I^{\prime};\kappa}^{\mu}f=\sum_{a\in\Gamma}\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\ .

Thus we have

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\Gamma}\left|\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|^{2}\left\|h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{a\in\Gamma}\left\|\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\sum_{a\in\Gamma}\left|\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|^{2}\ ,

for all
f\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
, which implies upon
taking
f=h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}
, that

\left\|h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{D}.

Now we compute the weighted Alpert Sobolev norms of indicators. By
independence of
\kappa
, we may assume
\kappa=1
. Using (
2.9
) and (
2.10
), we then note that

\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q}^{\mu}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\approx\frac{\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q}^{\mu}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\approx\frac{\frac{\left|I\right|_{\mu}^{2}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}=\left(\frac{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\right)^{2}\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }I\subset Q^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}\left(Q\right),

and hence from (
2.9
) we obtain

(2.16)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\varsupsetneqq I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\varsupsetneqq I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;1}^{\mu}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\approx\left|I\right|_{\mu}\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\varsupsetneqq I}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|I\right|_{\mu}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}2^{-2ns}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\pi^{n}I\right|_{\mu}}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|I\right|_{\mu}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}2^{-2ns}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\pi^{n}I\right|_{\mu}}.

Since
\mu
is doubling, it also satisfies a
dyadic reverse doubling

condition with reverse doubling exponent
\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}>0

depending on the doubling constant, i.e.

\left|\pi^{n}I\right|_{\mu}\geq c2^{n\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\left|I\right|_{\mu}.

Then for
s>-\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}
we have
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}2^{-2ns}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\pi^{n}I\right|_{\mu}}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}2^{-2n\left(s+\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}\right)}<\infty
, and so

\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\approx\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|I\right|_{\mu}\ .

Altogether we have proved the following lemma.

Lemma 24
.

Suppose
\mu
is a locally finite positive Borel measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\left\|h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-s},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }I\in\mathcal{D},a\in\Gamma_{I,n,\kappa},\kappa\geq 1\text{ and }s\in\mathbb{R},

and if
\mu
is a doubling measure,

\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}\approx\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\mu}},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }I\in\mathcal{D},\kappa\geq 1\text{ and }s>-\theta_{\mu}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}.

2.4.1.
Sharpness

Here we construct measures for which
\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\infty
for all intervals
I

and
s<0
, and thus are
not
dyadic reverse doubling. A trivial
example is any finite measure
\mu
, and an infinite example is
d\mu\left(x\right)=\mathbf{1}_{\left[e,\infty\right)}\left(x\right)\frac{1}{x\ln x}
. In fact we have the following lemma.

Lemma 25
.

If there is
s<0
such that

\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\leq C\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|I\right|_{\mu}

for all dyadic intervals
I
, then
\mu
is a dyadic reverse doubling
measure with exponent
\left|s\right|
.

Proof.

We have

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}2^{\left(n+1\right)\left|s\right|}\frac{1}{\left|\pi^{n}I\right|_{\mu}}\lesssim\left|I\right|_{\mu}^{-2}\ell\left(I\right)^{2s}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\leq C\left|I\right|_{\mu}^{-1},\text{ \ \ \ \ for all intervals }I\text{,}

which shows that
\left|\pi^{n}I\right|_{\mu}\geq\frac{1}{C}2^{\left(n+1\right)\left|s\right|}\left|I\right|_{\mu}
for all intervals
I
, which is the dyadic reverse doubling condition
with
t=\left|s\right|
.

Remark 26
.

There is an asymmetry inherent in the homogeneous Sobolev two weight
inequality (
3.1
) for general measures. If we wish to use

cube testing
to characterize the Sobolev inequality (
3.1
) for some
s>0

assuming
the estimate
\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\leq C\ell\left(I\right)^{2s}\left|I\right|_{\omega}
, then from the
equivalence with the bilinear inequality (
3.2
) and the
discussion and lemma above, we see that
\omega
needs to be restricted, in
fact by reverse dyadic doubling with exponent essentially greater than
2\left|s\right|
, while no restriction needs to be made on
\sigma
.

2.4.2.
Norms of moduli of Alpert wavelets

Let
\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}=\left(h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right)_{a\in\Gamma}
be the vector of Alpert wavelets associated with
the cube
J
. Note that for
\omega
doubling and
0\leq s<1
, we have

\frac{\left\|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}{\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}}=1,

and we now show that the same sort of Sobolev estimate holds for the
absolute value
\left|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right|
of
the vector Alpert wavelet (which remains trivial in the case
\kappa=1

since
\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}
is then constant on dyadic children
of
J
).

Lemma 27
.

Let
\mu
be a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
the modulus of a vector of Alpert wavelets
\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}=\left\{h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\}_{a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}}

satisfies

\frac{\left\|\left|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}}{\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}}\lesssim C,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }J\in\mathcal{D}.

Proof.

We expand

\left\|\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{Q}\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}.

For a cube
Q
contained in a child
J^{\prime}
of
J
that is disjoint
from the zero set of the polynomial
\mathbf{1}_{J^{\prime}}h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}
on the child
J^{\prime}
, the absolute values on the Alpert
wavelet can be removed, and we obtain that
\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|=\pm\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}
vanishes. On the other hand, if
Q\subset J^{\prime}
intersects the zero set
Z
of the polynomial
\mathbf{1}_{J^{\prime}}h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}
, then we use
\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\leq C\frac{\left|\widehat{f}\left(Q\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}}
to obtain the crude estimate,

\displaystyle\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a},\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|^{2}d\mu=\left|\left\langle h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a},\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\int_{Q}\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|^{2}d\mu\right)\left\|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left|Q\right|_{\mu}\lesssim\frac{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}{\left|J\right|_{\mu}},

and together with Corollary
9
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\subset J^{\prime}:\ Q\cap Z\neq\emptyset}\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\lesssim\sum_{Q\subset J^{\prime}:\ Q\cap Z\neq\emptyset}\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\frac{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}{\left|J\right|_{\mu}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\left(2^{-m}\ell\left(J\right)\right)^{2s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\subset J^{\prime}:\ Q\cap Z\neq\emptyset\\
\ell\left(Q\right)=2^{-m}\ell\left(J\right)\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}{\left|J\right|_{\mu}}\lesssim\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{-2ms}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q\subset J^{\prime}:\ Q\cap Z\neq\emptyset\\
\ell\left(Q\right)=2^{-m}\ell\left(J\right)\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}{\left|J\right|_{\mu}}\right)\ \ell\left(J\right)^{2s}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{-2ms}2^{-\varepsilon m}\right)\ \ell\left(J\right)^{2s}\ ,

which is the estimate we want when
s>0
or
-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}<s\leq 0
, where
\varepsilon=\varepsilon\left(\mu\right)>0
.

Finally, for big cubes
Q
containing
J
there is only the tower above
J

to consider, and trivial estimates work:

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\supset J}\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}:\ Q\supset J}\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\left|\left\langle h_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu,a},\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|^{2}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{2ms}\left|\left\langle h_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}J;\kappa}^{\mu,a},\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|^{2}\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}\lesssim\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{2ms}\left(\int_{J}\left|h_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}J;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|^{2}d\mu\right)\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{2ms}\frac{\left|J\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\pi^{\left(m\right)}J\right|_{\mu}}\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}\lesssim\ell\left(J\right)^{2s},

provided
s<0
or
s
is small enough depending on the doubling constant of
\mu
.

2.5.
Duality

Here we compute the dual space of
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)

under the
L^{2}\left(\mu\right)
pairing

\left\langle f,g\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f\left(x\right)g\left(x\right)d\mu\left(x\right)=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D},J\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}f\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\mu}gd\mu=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}f\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\mu}gd\mu.

Lemma 28
.

Let
-1<s<1
. Then

\left(W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)\right)^{\ast}=W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right),

holds in the sense that if
g\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)

then
f\rightarrow\left\langle f,g\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}

defines a bounded linear functional on
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
, and conversely that every bounded linear functional on
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
arises in this way.

Proof.

For
\kappa
sufficiently large, Cauchy-Schwarz gives

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle f,g\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f,\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}g\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|=\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right)\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}g\right)d\mu\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right)\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}g\right)d\mu\right|=\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right)\ell\left(I\right)^{s}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}g\right)d\mu\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right)\right|^{2}d\mu}\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\ell\left(I\right)^{s}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}g\right)\right|^{2}d\mu}=\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)}.

Conversely, if
\Lambda\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)^{\ast}
is a continuous linear functional on
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
, then for
\kappa
sufficiently large

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\ell\left(I\right)^{s}\Lambda h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\right|=\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\Lambda h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\right|=\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\Lambda\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right)\right|=\left|\Lambda f\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\Lambda\right\|\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}=\left\|\Lambda\right\|\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right)\right|^{2}d\mu}=\left\|\Lambda\right\|\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|^{2}}

for all choices of coefficients
\left\{\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\in\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{D}\right)
, and so we have
\ell\left(I\right)^{s}\Lambda h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\in\ell^{2}\left(\mathcal{D}\right)
, i.e.

\sqrt{\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{2s}\left|\Lambda h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}\right|^{2}}\leq\left\|\Lambda\right\|.

Thus if we define
g
to have Alpert coefficients
\Lambda h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}
, i.e.

g=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}\\
a\in\Gamma\end{subarray}}\left\langle g,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}\\
a\in\Gamma\end{subarray}}\left(\Lambda h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right)h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\ ,

then
g\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)
since

\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)}=\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left|\ell\left(I\right)^{s}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}g\right)\right|^{2}d\mu}=\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}\\
a\in\Gamma\end{subarray}}\ell\left(I\right)^{2s}\left|\left\langle g,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\right|^{2}}=\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}\\
a\in\Gamma\end{subarray}}\ell\left(I\right)^{2s}\left|\Lambda h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right|^{2}}\leq\left\|\Lambda\right\|<\infty,

and finally we have

\Lambda f=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}\\
a\in\Gamma\end{subarray}}\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\Lambda h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{D}\\
a\in\Gamma\end{subarray}}\left\langle f,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}\left\langle g,h_{I;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f\left(x\right)g\left(x\right)d\mu\left(x\right)=\left\langle f,g\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}.

2.6.
Quasiorthogonality in weighted Sobolev spaces

Let
\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu,s}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}
and
\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu,s}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}
.
Since
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu,s}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{D}}
is a complete set of orthogonal projections on
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)
, we have

\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu,s}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu,s}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\ ,

and then if
\left\{\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu,s}\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}
is a collection of projections, indexed by a subgrid
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{D}
satisfying an appropriate Carleson condition, we expect
to have

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu,s}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\ .

This inequality says we can replace the collection of moment vanishing
projections
\left\{\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\right\}_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}
with a collection of averaging projections
\left\{\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}
provided the subset
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathcal{D}
is sufficiently small that an appropriate
Carleson condition holds. Here is the quasiorthogonality lemma with
appropriate Carleson condition that is suitable for use with Sobolev
spaces,and in which
\left|f\right|
does not appear. Finally, it
can be viewed as a Sobolev space version of the Carleson Embedding Theorem.

Definition 29
.

A subgrid
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}
satisfies the
\varepsilon
-strong
\mu
-Carleson condition
if,

(2.17)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F\in\mathcal{F}\\
F\subset F^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)}{\ell\left(F\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|F\right|_{\mu}\leq C\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}.

Lemma 30

(Quasiorthogonality Lemma)
.

Let
\mu
be a doubling measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Suppose
that for some
\varepsilon>0
, the subgrid
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}

satisfies the
\varepsilon
-strong
\mu
-Carleson condition (
2.17
). Then for
s<\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
we have

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\ .

Proof.

Since
\frac{\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f}{\left\|\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{\infty}}
is a normalized polynomial of degree less than
\kappa
on the
\mathcal{D}
-child
\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)_{F}
of
\pi^{\left(m\right)}F
that contains
F
, we have by (
2.9
) and (
2.10
),

\left|F\right|_{\mu}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\approx\int_{F}\left|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right|^{2}d\mu\leq\int_{F}\left|\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right|^{2}d\mu\approx\left|F\right|_{\mu}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\ ,

and so for any
t<0
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left\|\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa_{1}}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\lesssim\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left|F\right|_{\mu}\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa}^{\mu}\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left|F\right|_{\mu}\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{\infty}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left|F\right|_{\mu}\left(\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\ell\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)^{2t}\right)\left(\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\ell\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)^{-2t}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{2t-2s}\left|F\right|_{\mu}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\ell\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)^{-2t}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}.

Substituting
F^{\prime}
for
\pi^{\left(m\right)}F
, and letting
t=s-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}<0
, we obtain from the
\varepsilon
-strong Carleson
condition (
2.17
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-2s}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\lesssim\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{2t-2s}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left|F\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)_{F}\right|_{\mu}}\ell\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)^{-2t}\int_{\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)_{F}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}d\mu

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F\right)^{2t-2s}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left|F\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)_{F}\right|_{\mu}}\ell\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)^{-2t}\int_{\left(\pi^{\left(m\right)}F\right)_{F}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{\pi^{\left(m\right)}F;\kappa}^{\mu}f\right|^{2}d\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F\in\mathcal{F}\\
F\subset F^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\ell\left(F\right)^{-\varepsilon}\frac{\left|F\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\left(F^{\prime}\right)_{F}\right|_{\mu}}\right)\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)^{\varepsilon-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{F^{\prime};\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\left(C\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)^{-\varepsilon}\frac{\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\mu}}{\left|\left(F^{\prime}\right)_{F}\right|_{\mu}}\right)\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)^{\varepsilon-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{F^{\prime};\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\lesssim\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{F^{\prime};\kappa}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\ .

Remark 31
.

We can replace
f
by its modulus
\left|f\right|
in the above
lemma when
\kappa=1
and
s>0
is sufficiently small. Indeed, by the
reverse triangle inequality we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left|f\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{Q}\left|\frac{f\left(x\right)-\mathbb{E}_{Q;1}^{\mu}f\left(x\right)}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}}\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)=\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{Q}\left|\frac{f\left(x\right)-\left(\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}}\int_{Q}fd\mu\right)\mathbf{1}_{Q}\left(x\right)}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}}\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{Q\in\mathbb{D}}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}^{2}}\int_{Q}\int_{Q}\left|\frac{\left|f\left(x\right)\right|-\left|f\left(y\right)\right|}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}}\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{Q\in\mathbb{D}}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\mu}^{2}}\int_{Q}\int_{Q}\left|\frac{f\left(x\right)-f\left(y\right)}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}}\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)=\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

and now we use the equivalence
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iff};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\approx\left\|\cdot\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad};1}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}
for
\left|s\right|

sufficiently small.

3.
Preliminaries: weighted Sobolev norm inequalities

Duality shows the equivalence of weighted norm inequalities with bilinear
inequalities.

Lemma 32
.

The Sobolev norm inequality

(3.1)

\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\leq\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{o}p}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\ ,

is equivalent to the bilinear inequality

(3.2)

\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|\leq\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}il}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,\text{ \ \ for }f,g\in L^{2}\left(\mu\right).

Proof.

Indeed, if the bilinear inequality holds and
f=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f
and
g=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g
, then

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right)gd\omega\right|=\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|\leq\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}il}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

shows that

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)^{\ast}}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right)gd\omega\right|=\sup_{\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\leq 1}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right)gd\omega\right|\leq\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}il}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{o}p}}\leq\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}il}}\text{ since }L^{2}\left(\mu\right)\text{ is dense in }W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right).

Conversely, if the norm inequality holds, then

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right)gd\omega\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)^{\ast}}\leq\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{o}p}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{o}p}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{b}il}}\leq\left\|T^{\alpha}\right\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{o}p}}.

3.1.
The good-bad decomposition

Here we follow the random grid idea of Nazarov, Treil and Volberg. Denote by

\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}
the collection of all dyadic grids
\mathcal{D}
.
For a weight
\mu
, we consider a random choice of dyadic grid
\mathcal{D}

on the natural probability space
\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}
.

Definition 33
.

For a positive integer
r
and
0<\varepsilon<1
, a cube
J\in\mathcal{D}
is said to be

\left(r\emph{,}\varepsilon\right)
-bad
if there is a cube
I\in\mathcal{D}
with
\lvert I\rvert\geq 2^{r}\lvert J\rvert
, and

\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}(e(I),J)\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\lvert J\rvert^{\varepsilon}\lvert I\rvert^{1-\varepsilon}\,.

Here,
e(J)
is the union of the boundaries of the children of the cube
J
.
(This contains the set of discontinuities of
h_{J;\kappa}^{\mu}
and its
derivatives less than order
\kappa
.) Otherwise,
J
is said to be
\left(r\emph{,}\varepsilon\right)
-
{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
.

The basic proposition here is this, see e.g.
[
Vol
]
and e.g.
[
LaWi
]

or
[
SaShUr7
]
for higher dimensions.

Proposition 34
.

There is the conditional probability estimate

\mathbb{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ond}}^{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}\left(J\text{ is }\left(r\emph{,}\varepsilon\right)\text{-}{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}}:J\in\mathcal{D}\right)\leq C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\varepsilon r}.

Define projections

(3.3)

\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{D}}^{\mu}f=\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}}^{\mu}f\equiv\sum_{I\text{ is }\left(r,\varepsilon\right)\text{-}{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\text{ }\in\mathcal{D}}\Delta_{I}^{\mu}f\,\text{\ and }\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\mu}f=\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}}}^{\mu}f\equiv f-\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}}^{\mu}f.

Recall that

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{Q\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }\mathcal{D}\in\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}.

The basic Proposition is then this.

Proposition 35
.

(cf. Theorem 17.1 in
[
Vol
]
where the middle line below
is treated) We have the estimates

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}r}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\frac{\varepsilon r}{p}}\left\|f\right\|_{L^{p}\left(\mu\right)},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{W_{\mathcal{D}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}r}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\left(\left\|\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}\text{ is }\left(r,\varepsilon\right)\text{-}{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad}}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\langle f,h_{I}^{\mu}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}2^{-\varepsilon r}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left\langle f,h_{I}^{\mu}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}=C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\varepsilon r}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\,,

and then

\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\left(\left\|\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}\right)\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\left(\left\|\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\mu}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}^{2}\right)}\leq C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}r}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)}\ .

Similarly for
L^{p}\left(\mu\right)
and
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\mu\right)
in place of
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
.

From this we conclude the following: Given any
0<\varepsilon<1
, there is
a choice of
r
, depending on
\varepsilon
, so that the following holds.
Let
T:W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)\rightarrow W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)
be a bounded linear operator, where for
specificity we take
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}=W_{\mathcal{D}_{0}}^{s}
, and
\mathcal{D}_{0}
is the standard dyadic grid on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We then
have

(3.4)

\left\|T\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)\rightarrow W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\leq 2\sup_{\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}=1}\sup_{\left\|\phi\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}=1}\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{E}}\lvert\left\langle T\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{D}}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{D}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\rvert\,.

Indeed, we can choose
f\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
of
norm one, and
g\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)
of norm one,
and we can write

f=\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{D}}^{\sigma}f+\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\sigma}f\,

and similarly for
g
and
\mathcal{E}
, so that

\displaystyle\left\|T\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)\rightarrow W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}=\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{E}}\lvert\left\langle T_{\sigma}\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{D}}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{E}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\rvert+\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{E}}\lvert\left\langle T_{\sigma}\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{E}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\rvert

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{E}}\lvert\left\langle T_{\sigma}\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{D}}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{E}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\rvert+\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{E}}\lvert\left\langle T_{\sigma}\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{D}}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{b}ad};}\mathcal{E}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}}^{\mathcal{E}}\lvert\left\langle T_{\sigma}\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{D}}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood};}\mathcal{E}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\rvert+3C_{\varepsilon}2^{-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}r}\left\|T\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)\rightarrow W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

And this proves (
3.4
) for
r
sufficiently large.

This has the following implication for us:
Given any linear operator
T
and
0<\varepsilon<1
, it suffices to consider only
\left(\emph{r,}\varepsilon\right)
-
{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
cubes for r sufficiently large, and prove an estimate for
\left\|T\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)\rightarrow W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}
that is independent of this assumption.

Accordingly, we will call
\left(r\emph{,}\varepsilon\right)
-
{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
cubes just
{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
cubes
from now on.
At certain points in the arguments below, such as in the treatment of the
neighbour form for
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
, we will need
to further restrict the parameter
\varepsilon
(and accordingly
r
as
well).

3.2.
Defining the norm inequality

We now turn to a precise definition of the weighted norm inequality

(3.5)

\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\leq\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)},\ \ \ \ \ f\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right),

where
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
is the Hilbert space
completion of the space of functions
f\in L_{\mathop{\mathrm{l}oc}}^{2}\left(\sigma\right)
for which

\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}<\infty.

A similar definition holds for
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)
.
For a precise definition of (
3.5
), it is possible to proceed
with the notion of associating operators and kernels through an identity for
functions with disjoint support as in
[
Ste2
]
. However, we choose to
follow the approach in
[
SaShUr9
, see page 314]
. So we suppose that
K^{\alpha}
is a smooth
\alpha
-fractional Calderón-Zygmund kernel,
and we introduce a family
\left\{\eta_{\delta,R}^{\alpha}\right\}_{0<\delta<R<\infty}
of nonnegative functions on
\left[0,\infty\right)
so that the truncated kernels
K_{\delta,R}^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)=\eta_{\delta,R}^{\alpha}\left(\left|x-y\right|\right)K^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)
are bounded with compact support for fixed
x

or
y
, and uniformly satisfy (
1.2
). Then the truncated
operators

T_{\sigma,\delta,R}^{\alpha}f\left(x\right)\equiv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}K_{\delta,R}^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)f\left(y\right)d\sigma\left(y\right),\ \ \ \ \ x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

are pointwise well-defined, and we will refer to the pair
\left(K^{\alpha},\left\{\eta_{\delta,R}^{\alpha}\right\}_{0<\delta<R<\infty}\right)

as an
\alpha
-fractional singular integral operator, which we typically
denote by
T^{\alpha}
, suppressing the dependence on the truncations.

Definition 36
.

We say that an
\alpha
-fractional singular integral
operator
T^{\alpha}=\left(K^{\alpha},\left\{\eta_{\delta,R}^{\alpha}\right\}_{0<\delta<R<\infty}\right)
satisfies the norm inequality (
3.5
) provided

\left\|T_{\sigma,\delta,R}^{\alpha}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\leq\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)},\ \ \ \ \ f\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right),0<\delta<R<\infty.

Independence of Truncations:

In the presence of
the classical Muckenhoupt condition
A_{2}^{\alpha}
, the norm inequality (
3.5
) is essentially independent of the choice of truncations
used, including
nonsmooth
truncations as well - see
[
LaSaShUr3
]
.
However, in dealing with the Monotonicity Lemma
44
below, where
\kappa^{th}
order Taylor approximations are made on the truncated kernels,
it is necessary to use sufficiently smooth truncations. Similar comments
apply to the Cube Testing conditions (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) below.

3.2.1.
Ellipticity of kernels

Modifying slightly the definition in
[
Ste
, (39) on page 210]
, we say
that an
\alpha
-fractional Calderón-Zygmund kernel
K^{\alpha}
is

elliptic in the sense of Stein
if there is a unit coordinate vector
\mathbf{e}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some
1\leq k\leq n
, and a positive
constant
c>0
such that

\left|K^{\alpha}\left(x,x+t\mathbf{e}_{k}\right)\right|\geq c\left|t\right|^{\alpha-n},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all }t\in\mathbb{R}.

For example, the Beurling, Cauchy and Riesz transform kernels, as well as
those for
k
-iterated Riesz transforms are elliptic in the sense of Stein
for any
k\geq 1
.

3.2.2.
Cube testing

While the next more general testing conditions with
\kappa>1
, introduced
in
[
RaSaWi
]
and
[
Saw6
]
, are not used in the statements of our
theorems, they will be used in the course of our proof.

The
\kappa
-cube testing conditions
associated with an
\alpha
-fractional singular integral operator
T^{\alpha}
, introduced in
[
RaSaWi
]
for
s=0
, are given by

(3.6)

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}\max_{0\leq\left|\beta\right|<\kappa}\frac{1}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{Q}T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{Q}m_{Q}^{\beta}\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}<\infty,

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{T}_{\left(T^{\alpha}\right)^{\ast}}^{\kappa,-s}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}\max_{0\leq\left|\beta\right|<\kappa}\frac{1}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\left|Q\right|_{\omega}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{Q}T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\left(\mathbf{1}_{Q}m_{Q}^{\beta}\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}<\infty,

where
\left(T^{\alpha,\ast}\right)_{\omega}=\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\right)^{\ast}
, with
m_{Q}^{\beta}\left(x\right)\equiv\left(\frac{x-c_{Q}}{\ell\left(Q\right)}\right)^{\beta}
for any cube
Q
and
multiindex
\beta
, where
c_{Q}
is the center of the cube
Q
, and where
we interpret the right hand sides as holding uniformly over all sufficiently
smooth truncations of
T^{\alpha}
. Equivalently, in the presence of
A_{2}^{\alpha}
, we can take a single suitable truncation, see Independence
of Truncations in Subsubsection
Independence of Truncations:
above.

We also use the larger
triple
\kappa
-cube testing conditions

in which the integrals over
Q
are extended to the triple
3Q
of
Q
:

(3.7)

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}\max_{0\leq\left|\beta\right|<\kappa}\frac{1}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{-2s}\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{3Q}T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{Q}m_{Q}^{\beta}\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}<\infty,

\displaystyle\left(\mathfrak{TR}_{\left(T^{\alpha}\right)^{\ast}}^{\kappa,-s}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}\max_{0\leq\left|\beta\right|<\kappa}\frac{1}{\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\left|Q\right|_{\omega}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{3Q}T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\left(\mathbf{1}_{Q}m_{Q}^{\beta}\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}<\infty.

3.3.
Necessity of the classical Muckenhoupt condition

Suppose that
T^{\alpha}f=K\ast f
where
K^{\alpha}\left(x\right)=\frac{\Omega\left(x\right)}{\left|x\right|^{n-\alpha}}
, and
\Omega\left(x\right)
is homogeneous of degree
0
and smooth away from the
origin. Note that we do not require any cancellation properties on
\Omega
, except that when
\alpha=0
we suppose
\int\Omega\left(x\right)d\sigma_{n-1}\left(x\right)=0
where
\sigma_{n-1}
is surface measure
on the sphere (see e.g.
[
Ste
]
page 68 for the case
\alpha=0
). We
assume
\Omega
is nontrivial in the sense that there is a coordinate
direction
\Theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
such that
\Omega\left(\Theta\right)\neq 0
. Then there is a cone
\Gamma
centered on
\Theta
on
which
K\left(x\right)=\frac{\Omega\left(x\right)}{\left|x\right|^{n-\alpha}}
and
\Omega\left(x\right)\geq c>0
for
x\in\Gamma
. Consider pairs of separated dyadic cubes in direction
\Theta
,

\mathcal{SP}_{\Theta}\equiv\left\{\left(I,I^{\prime}\right):\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,I^{\prime}\right)\approx\ell\left(I\right)=\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)\text{, }R_{1}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,I^{\prime}\right)\approx R\ell\left(I\right)\text{, and }I^{\prime}\text{ has direction }\Theta\text{ from }I\right\},

where
R
is chosen large enough that if the cone
\Gamma
is translated to
any point in
I
, then it contains any cube
I^{\prime}
for which
\left(I,I^{\prime}\right)\in\mathcal{SP}_{\Theta}
.

We first derive the ‘separated’ Muckenhoupt condition from the full testing
condition for
T^{\alpha}
, i.e.

\left(\frac{1}{\left|I^{\prime}\right|}\int_{I^{\prime}}d\omega\right)\left(\frac{1}{\left|I\right|}\int_{I}d\sigma\right)\leq\mathfrak{FT}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(s;\sigma,\omega\right)^{2},\ \ \ \ \ \left(I,I^{\prime}\right)\in\mathcal{SP}_{\Theta}\text{.}

We may assume without loss of generality that
\Theta=e_{1}
, the unit
vector in the direction of the positive
x_{1}
-axis. Now we choose a
special unit Haar function
h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}
, i.e.
\bigtriangleup_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}=h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}

and
\left\|h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}=1
, satisfying

h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)=\sum_{K\in\mathfrak{C}\left(I\right)}a_{K}\mathbf{1}_{K}\left(x\right)\text{, where }\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}a_{K}>0&\text{ if }&K\text{ lies to the right of center}\\
a_{K}<0&\text{ if }&K\text{ lies to the left of center}\end{array}\right.,

where for a cube
Q
centered at the origin, we say a child
K
lies to the
right of center if
K
is contained in the half space where
x_{1}\geq 0
.
We now compute

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I^{\prime}}T^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I^{\prime}}T^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\geq\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}\left(T^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|\left\langle T^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma,h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|^{2}=\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|\int_{I^{\prime}}\left(\int_{I}K^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|\int_{I^{\prime}}\left(\int_{I}\left[K^{\alpha}\left(x-y\right)-K^{\alpha}\left(c_{I^{\prime}}-y\right)\right]d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|\int_{I^{\prime}}\int_{I}\left[K^{\alpha}\left(x-y\right)-K^{\alpha}\left(c_{I^{\prime}}-y\right)\right]h_{I^{\prime}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\sigma\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle c\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left(\int_{I^{\prime}}\int_{I}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}}\frac{\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,I^{\prime}\right)^{n+1-\alpha}}\right|d\sigma\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right)^{2}=c\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}^{2}\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\omega}}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,I^{\prime}\right)^{2n-2\alpha}},

since
\omega
is doubling. Thus we have

\displaystyle\ell\left(I^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}^{2}\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\omega}}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,I^{\prime}\right)^{2n-2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I^{\prime}}T^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{FT}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(s;\sigma,\omega\right)^{2}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}\approx\mathfrak{FT}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(s;\sigma,\omega\right)^{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\ ,

which gives the desired inequality,

\frac{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I\right|^{n-\alpha}}\frac{\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|I^{\prime}\right|^{n-\alpha}}\lesssim\mathfrak{FT}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(s;\sigma,\omega\right)^{2}.

Now using
[
LaSaUr1
]
or
[
SaShUr7
]
, we can derive from this the full
Muckenhoupt inequality when
\omega
and
\sigma
contain no common point
masses, which is certainly the case for doubling measures,

A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)=\sup_{I}\frac{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\left|I\right|_{\omega}}{\left|I\right|^{2\left(n-\alpha\right)}}\lesssim\mathfrak{FT}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(s;\sigma,\omega\right)^{2}.

Thus we have proved the following lemma.

Lemma 37
.

If
T^{\alpha}f=K^{\alpha}\ast f
where
K^{\alpha}\left(x\right)=\frac{\Omega\left(x\right)}{\left|x\right|^{n-\alpha}}
, and
\Omega\left(x\right)
is homogeneous of degree
0
and smooth
away from the origin and
\Omega\left(\mathbf{e}_{k}\right)\neq 0
for
some
1\leq k\leq n
, then boundedness of
T^{\alpha}
from
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
to
W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)
,
implies the
A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
condition, more
precisely,

\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}\lesssim\mathfrak{FT}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(s;\sigma,\omega\right)\leq\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(s;\sigma,\omega\right).

3.4.
Necessity of the strong
\kappa^{th}
order Pivotal
Condition with full grids and doubling weights

We will need the following definitions that capture a critical property of
stopping times arising from pivotal criteria.

Definition 38
.

Let
\sigma
be a measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
0\leq\beta<1
.

(1)

We say that a collection of dyadic cubes
\left\{F_{r}\right\}_{r=1}^{\infty}
is a
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-subdecomposition of a
dyadic cube
F
if
F_{r}\subset F
for all
1<r<\infty
and

\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left|F_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}}\right)^{1-\beta}\leq C.

(2)

We say that a grid
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}
is
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-full
if
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)

is a
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-subdecomposition of
F
, uniformly over
all
F\in\mathcal{F}
(with respect to a constant
C
we suppress).

The smaller fractional Poisson integrals
\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(Q,\mu\right)
used here, in
[
RaSaWi
]
and elsewhere, are given
by

(3.8)

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(Q,\mu\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)^{\kappa}}{\left(\ell\left(Q\right)+\left|y-c_{Q}\right|\right)^{n+\kappa-\alpha}}d\mu\left(y\right),\ \ \ \ \ \kappa\geq 1,

and the
\kappa^{th}
-order fractional pivotal constants
\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,\kappa},\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,\kappa,\ast}<\infty
,
\kappa\geq 1
, are given by

(3.9)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\supset\dot{\cup}Q_{r}}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,\kappa,\ast}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\supset\dot{\cup}Q_{r}}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\omega}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q}\omega\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\sigma}=\left(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\right)^{2}\ ,

and for
0<\beta,\varepsilon\leq 1
, the
\left(\beta,\varepsilon\right)
-strong
\kappa
-pivotal constants
\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa},\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa,\ast}<\infty
,
\kappa\geq 1
are given by

(3.10)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\supset\dot{\cup}Q_{r}\ \text{is }\left(\beta,\sigma\right)\text{-full}}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q}\sigma\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)}{\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa,\ast}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\supset\dot{\cup}Q_{r}\ \text{is }\left(\beta,\sigma\right)\text{-full}}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\omega}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q}\omega\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)}{\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\sigma}=\left(\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\right)^{2}\ ,

and where the suprema are taken over all subdecompositions of a cube
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}
into pairwise disjoint dyadic subcubes
Q_{r}
for which
\left\{Q_{r}\right\}_{r=1}^{\infty}
is a
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-subdecomposition of
Q
.

Lemma 39
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
be as in (
3.15
), and
0\leq\alpha<n
. Let
\gamma>1
and suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a stopping time
grid constructed from a pair of doubling measures
\sigma,\omega
using
the stopping criterion,

(3.11)

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{F}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|I\right|_{\omega}\geq\gamma\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{D}\left(F\right),

i.e. for each
F\in\mathcal{F}
, the set
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
of
\mathcal{F}
-children of
F
consists of the maximal dyadic
subcubes
I
of
F
satisfying (
3.11
). Then
\mathcal{F}
is
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-full provided
\kappa>\frac{n}{2}
and
\beta,\varepsilon\
are sufficiently small. More precisely, under these
conditions we have

\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left|F_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}}\right)^{1-\beta}\lesssim A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{1-\beta}.

Proof.

Suppose
\left\{F_{r}\right\}_{r=1}^{\infty}=\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)
is the set of stopping children of
F
in the grid
\mathcal{F}
. Then by maximality of the cubes
F_{r}
, they also satisfy

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(\pi_{\mathcal{D}}F_{r},\mathbf{1}_{F}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|\pi_{\mathcal{D}}F_{r}\right|_{\omega}<\gamma\left|\pi_{\mathcal{D}}F_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\ ,

and since both measures are doubling, we have

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F_{r},\mathbf{1}_{F}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|F_{r}\right|_{\omega}\approx\left|F_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\ .

Thus the inequality
\lesssim
in the above equivalence is from the
stopping criterion, while the opposite inequality
\gtrsim
is from
doubling. Using doubling once more it follows easily that

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F_{r},\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus\gamma F_{r}}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|F_{r}\right|_{\omega}\approx\left|F_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\ .

Now using (
3.15
), namely

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}(J,\sigma\mathbf{1}_{K\setminus I})\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{\kappa-\varepsilon\left(n+m-\alpha\right)}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}(I,\sigma\mathbf{1}_{K\setminus I}),

we have

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left[\frac{\left|F_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}}\right]^{1-\beta}\approx\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left[\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F_{r},\mathbf{1}_{F\setminus\gamma F_{r}}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|F_{r}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}}\right]^{1-\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left[\left(\frac{\ell\left(F_{r}\right)}{\ell\left(F\right)}\right)^{2\kappa-2\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}(F,\sigma\mathbf{1}_{F})^{2}\frac{\left|F_{r}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}}\right]^{1-\beta}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left[\left(\frac{\ell\left(F_{r}\right)}{\ell\left(F\right)}\right)^{2\kappa-2\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)}\frac{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\left|F_{r}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|F\right|^{2-2\alpha}}\right]^{1-\beta}\lesssim\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left[\left(\frac{\ell\left(F_{r}\right)}{\ell\left(F\right)}\right)^{2\kappa-2\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)+\theta_{\omega}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\frac{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|F\right|^{2-2\alpha}}\right]^{1-\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left[\left(\frac{\ell\left(F_{r}\right)}{\ell\left(F\right)}\right)^{2\kappa-2\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)+\theta_{\omega}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right]^{1-\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{1-\beta}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F_{r}\right)}{\ell\left(F\right)}\right)^{\left[2\kappa-2\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)+\theta_{\omega}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}\right]\left(1-\beta\right)}\lesssim A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{1-\beta},

provided

\left[2\kappa-2\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)+\theta_{\omega}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}\right]\left(1-\beta\right)\geq n,

which in turn holds for any
\kappa>\frac{n}{2}
and
\theta_{\omega}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}>0
if
\varepsilon
and
\beta
are chosen sufficiently
small. Thus we conclude that
\left\{F_{r}\right\}_{r=1}^{\infty}
is a
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-subdecomposition of
F
for every
F\in\mathcal{F}
, which completes the proof that
\mathcal{F}
is
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-full.

The following lemma is a variant of one in
[
Saw6
, Subsection 4.1 on pages
12-13, especially Remark 15]
, where it was the point of departure for
freeing the theory from reliance on energy conditions when the measures are
doubling. This variant exploits the critical notion of a full grid.

Lemma 40
.

Let
0\leq\alpha<n
and
0<\varepsilon<1
and suppose
\sigma
is a doubling measure satisfying (
2.3
), i.e.

\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\ .

Then for
\kappa>\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}+\alpha-n
and
\beta=\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}
, we have

\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\leq C_{\kappa,\alpha,n,\varepsilon,\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}},\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}.

Proof.

A doubling measure
\sigma
has a ‘doubling exponent’
\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}>0
and a positive constant
c
that satisfies the condition
(see e.g.
[
Saw6
]
),

\left|2^{-j}Q\right|_{\sigma}\geq c2^{-j\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}}\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \text{for all
}j\in\mathbb{N}.

We can then exploit the doubling exponents
\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
and reverse doubling exponents
\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}
of the
doubling measure
\sigma
in order to derive certain
\kappa^{th}
order
pivotal conditions
\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)<\infty
. Indeed, if
\sigma
has
doubling exponent
\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
and
\kappa>\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}+\alpha-n
, we have

(3.12)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus I}\frac{\ell\left(I\right)^{\kappa}}{\left(\ell\left(I\right)+\left|x-c_{I}\right|\right)^{n+\kappa-\alpha}}d\sigma\left(x\right)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\ell\left(I\right)^{\alpha-n}\int_{2^{j}I\setminus 2^{j-1}I}\frac{1}{\left(1+\frac{\left|x-c_{I}\right|}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{n+\kappa-\alpha}}d\sigma\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|I\right|^{\frac{\alpha}{n}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-j\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)}\left|2^{j}I\right|_{\sigma}\lesssim\left|I\right|^{\frac{\alpha}{n}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}2^{-j\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)}\frac{1}{c2^{-j\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}}}\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\leq C_{n,\kappa,\alpha,\beta,\gamma}\left|I\right|^{\frac{\alpha}{n}-1}\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\ ,

provided
n+\kappa-\alpha-\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}>0
, i.e.
\kappa>\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}+\alpha-n
. It follows that if
I\supset\overset{\cdot}{\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}}_{r=1}^{\infty}I_{r}
is a
subdecomposition of
I
into pairwise disjoint cubes
I_{r}
, and
\kappa>\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}+\alpha-n
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}\lesssim\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\left|I_{r}\right|^{\frac{\alpha}{n}-1}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\frac{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|I_{r}\right|^{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{n}\right)}}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\lesssim A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\ .

Now
\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}\lesssim\frac{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}
, and so we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}\right)^{\frac{\varepsilon}{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}

\displaystyle A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}\right)^{\frac{\varepsilon}{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}}\frac{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\left|I\right|_{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left|I_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\right)^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}}\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\leq CA_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\;,

for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small, if we assume in addition that
\left\{I_{r}\right\}_{r=1}^{\infty}
is a
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-subdecomposition of
F
with
\beta=\frac{\varepsilon}{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}
.

This then gives,

(3.13)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{V}_{2,\frac{\varepsilon}{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}},\frac{\varepsilon}{\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\{I\right\}\cup\left\{I_{r}\right\}_{r=1}^{\infty}\text{ is }\left(\beta,\sigma\right)\text{-full}}\frac{1}{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(I_{r},\mathbf{1}_{I}\sigma\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(I_{r}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|I_{r}\right|_{\omega}\lesssim A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ ,

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }\kappa

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}+\alpha-n\text{ and }\varepsilon>0\text{ sufficiently small}\ .

A similar result holds for
\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa,\ast}
if
\kappa+n-\alpha>\theta_{\omega}^{\mathop{\mathrm{d}oub}}
and
\varepsilon>0
is sufficiently small, where
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{\theta_{\omega}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}
.

3.5.
The energy lemma

For
0\leq\alpha<n
and
m\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we recall from (
3.8
) the
m^{th}
-order fractional Poisson integral

\mathrm{P}_{m}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)\equiv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)^{m}}{\left(\ell\left(J\right)+\left|y-c_{J}\right|\right)^{m+n-\alpha}}d\mu\left(y\right),

where
\mathrm{P}_{1}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)=\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)
is the standard Poisson integral. The case
s=0
of
the following extension of the ‘energy lemma’ is due to Rahm, Sawyer and
Wick
[
RaSaWi
]
, and is proved in detail in
[
Saw6
, Lemmas 28 and 29 on
pages 27-30]
.

Definition 41
.

Given a subset
\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{D}
, define the projection
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\omega}\equiv\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}}\bigtriangleup_{J^{\prime};\kappa}^{\omega}
, and given a cube
J\in\mathcal{D}
, define the projection
\mathsf{P}_{J}^{\omega}\equiv\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}:\ J^{\prime}\subset J}\bigtriangleup_{J^{\prime};\kappa}^{\omega}
.

Lemma 42

(
Energy Lemma
)
.

Fix
\kappa\geq 1
. Let
J\
be a cube in
\mathcal{D}
, and let
\Psi_{J}\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)
be supported in
J
with
vanishing
\omega
-means up to order less than
\kappa
. Let
\nu
be a
positive measure supported in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J
with
\gamma>1
, and let
T^{\alpha}
be a smooth
\alpha
-fractional singular
integral operator with
0\leq\alpha<n
. Then for
\left|s\right|

sufficiently small, we have the ‘pivotal’ bound

(3.14)

\left|\left\langle T^{\alpha}\left(\varphi\nu\right),\Psi_{J}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\right|\lesssim C_{\gamma}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\nu\right)\ell\left(J\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\left\|\Psi_{J}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

for any function
\varphi
with
\left|\varphi\right|\leq 1
.

We also recall from
[
Saw6
, Lemma 33]
the following Poisson estimate,
that is a straightforward extension of the case
m=1
due to Nazarov, Treil
and Volberg in
[
NTV4
]
.

Lemma 43
.

Fix
m\geq 1
. Suppose that
J\subset I\subset K

and that
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,\partial I\right)>2\sqrt{n}\ell\left(J\right)^{\varepsilon}\ell\left(I\right)^{1-\varepsilon}
. Then

(3.15)

\mathrm{P}_{m}^{\alpha}(J,\sigma\mathbf{1}_{K\setminus I})\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{m-\varepsilon\left(n+m-\alpha\right)}\mathrm{P}_{m}^{\alpha}(I,\sigma\mathbf{1}_{K\setminus I}).

We now give Sobolev modifications to several known arguments. The next lemma
was proved in
[
RaSaWi
]
for
s=0
.

Lemma 44
.

Let
0\leq\alpha<n
,
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\delta<1
. Suppose that
\ I
and
J
are cubes in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
J\subset 2J\subset I
, and that
\mu
is a signed measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
supported outside
I
. Finally suppose that
T^{\alpha}
is a smooth
fractional singular integral on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with kernel
K^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)=K_{y}^{\alpha}\left(x\right)
, and that
\omega
is
a locally finite positive Borel measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

(3.16)

\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}T^{\alpha}\mu\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\lesssim\Phi_{\kappa,s}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)^{2}+\Psi_{\kappa,s}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\mu\right|\right)^{2},

where for a measure
\nu
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\kappa,s}^{\alpha}\left(J,\nu\right)^{2}\equiv\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\kappa\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)d\nu\left(y\right)\right|^{2}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}x^{\beta}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\ ,

\displaystyle\Psi_{\kappa,s}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\nu\right|\right)^{2}\equiv\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa+\delta}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\nu\right|\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\frac{\kappa}{n}}}\right)^{2}\left\|\left|x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right|^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mathbf{1}_{J}\omega\right)}^{2}\frac{\left\|\left|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}{\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}}\ ,

\displaystyle\text{where }m_{J}^{\kappa}\in J\text{ satisfies }\left\|\left|x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right|^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mathbf{1}_{J}\omega\right)}^{2}=\inf_{m\in J}\left\|\left|x-m\right|^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mathbf{1}_{J}\omega\right)}^{2}.

Remark 45
.

Note that when
s=0
, we have
\frac{\left\|\left|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}{\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}}=\frac{\left\|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}{1}=1
,
and so the above inequality becomes the familiar Monotonicity Lemma. For
s

close to zero, Lemma
27
shows that
\frac{\left\|\left|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}{\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}}\lesssim 1
, which gives the same familiar form.

Proof of Lemma
44
.

The proof is an easy adaptation of the one-dimensional proof in
[
RaSaWi
]
, which was in turn adapted from the proofs in
[
LaWi
]
and
[
SaShUr7
]
, but using a
\kappa^{th}
order Taylor expansion instead of a first order
expansion on the kernel
\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)\left(x\right)=K^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)
. Due to the importance of this lemma, as
explained above, we repeat the short argument.

Let
\left\{h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\}_{a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}}

be an orthonormal basis of
L_{J;\kappa}^{2}\left(\omega\right)

consisting of Alpert functions as above. Now we use the Calderón-Zygmund
smoothness estimate (
1.2
), together with Taylor’s
formula

\displaystyle K_{y}^{\alpha}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{T}ay}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)\left(x,c\right)+\frac{1}{\kappa!}\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(\theta\left(x,c\right)\right)\left(x-c\right)^{\beta};

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{T}ay}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)\left(x,c\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle K_{y}^{\alpha}\left(c\right)+\left[\left(x-c\right)\cdot\nabla\right]K_{y}^{\alpha}\left(c\right)+...+\frac{1}{\left(\kappa-1\right)!}\left[\left(x-c\right)\cdot\nabla\right]^{\kappa-1}K_{y}^{\alpha}\left(c\right),

and the vanishing means of the Alpert functions
h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}

for
a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}
, to obtain

\displaystyle\left\langle T^{\alpha}\mu,h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}K^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right\}d\mu\left(y\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\langle K_{y}^{\alpha},h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}d\mu\left(y\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\langle K_{y}^{\alpha}\left(x\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{T}ay}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)\left(x,m_{J}^{\kappa}\right),h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\left(x\right)\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}d\mu\left(y\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left\langle\frac{1}{\kappa!}\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(\theta\left(x,m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)\right)\left(x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)^{\beta},h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\left(x\right)\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}d\mu\left(y\right)\ \ \ \ \ \text{(some }\theta\left(x,m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)\in J\text{) }

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left\langle\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{\kappa!}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)d\mu\left(y\right)\right]\left(x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)^{\beta},h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left\langle\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{\kappa!}\left[\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(\theta\left(x,m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)\right)-\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)\right]d\mu\left(y\right)\right]\left(x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)^{\beta},h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

Then using that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)d\mu\left(y\right)
is
independent of
x\in J
, and that
\left\langle\left(x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)^{\beta},\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}=\left\langle x^{\beta},\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}
by moment vanishing
of the Alpert wavelets, we can continue with

\displaystyle\left\langle T^{\alpha}\mu,h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}=\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{\kappa!}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)d\mu\left(y\right)\right]\cdot\left\langle x^{\beta},h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ +\frac{1}{\kappa!}\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left\langle\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left[\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(\theta\left(x,m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)\right)-\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)\right]d\mu\left(y\right)\right]\left(x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)^{\beta},h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

Hence

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle T^{\alpha}\mu,h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}-\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{\kappa!}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)d\mu\left(y\right)\right]\cdot\left\langle x^{\beta},h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa!}\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left|\left\langle\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sup_{\theta\in J}\left|\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(\theta\right)-\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)\right|d\left|\mu\right|\left(y\right)\right]\left|x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right|^{\kappa},\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right|\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\|C_{CZ}\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa+\delta}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\mu\right|\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\kappa}}\left|x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right|^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}\left\|\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C_{CZ}\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa+\delta}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\mu\right|\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\kappa}}\left\|\left|x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right|^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mathbf{1}_{J}\omega\right)}\left\|\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

where in the last line we have used

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\sup_{\theta\in J}\left|\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(\theta\right)-\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)\right|d\left|\mu\right|\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C_{CZ}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\frac{\left|J\right|}{\left|y-c_{J}\right|}\right)^{\delta}\frac{d\left|\mu\right|\left(y\right)}{\left|y-c_{J}\right|^{\kappa+1-\alpha}}=C_{CZ}\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa+\delta}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\mu\right|\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\kappa}}.

Thus with
v_{J}^{\beta}=\frac{1}{\kappa!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}^{\kappa}\right)d\mu\left(y\right)
, and noting that the functions
\left\{v_{J}^{\beta}h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\}_{a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}}
are
orthonormal in
a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}
for each
\beta
and
J
, we
have

\displaystyle\left|v_{J}^{\beta}\left\langle x^{\beta},h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}}\left|\left\langle x^{\beta},v_{J}^{\beta}\cdot h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\right|^{2}=\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}v_{J}^{\beta}x^{\beta}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|v_{J}^{\beta}\right|^{2}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}x^{\beta}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}=\left|v_{J}^{\beta}\right|^{2}\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}x^{\beta}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\ ,

and hence

\displaystyle\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}T^{\alpha}\mu\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}=\left|\widehat{T^{\alpha}\mu}\left(J;\kappa\right)\right|^{2}\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}=\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}\sum_{a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}}\left|\left\langle T^{\alpha}\mu,h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left|v_{J}^{\beta}\right|^{2}\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}x^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}
