\displaystyle+O\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa+\delta}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\mu\right|\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\frac{\kappa}{n}}}\right)^{2}\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}\left\|\left|x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right|^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mathbf{1}_{J}\omega\right)}^{2}\sum_{a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}}\left\|\left|h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}.

Thus we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}T^{\alpha}\mu\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left|\frac{1}{\kappa!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}\right)d\mu\left(y\right)\right|^{2}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}x^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{2}\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa+\delta}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\mu\right|\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\frac{\kappa}{n}}}\right)^{2}\left\|\left|x-m_{J}^{\kappa}\right|^{\kappa}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mathbf{1}_{J}\omega\right)}^{2}\frac{\left\|\left|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}{\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}}\ ,

where
\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\equiv\left\{h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\}_{a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}}
and

\sum_{\left|\beta\right|=\kappa}\left|\frac{1}{\kappa!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(K_{y}^{\alpha}\right)^{\left(\beta\right)}\left(m_{J}\right)d\mu\left(y\right)\right|^{2}\lesssim\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\mu\right|\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\frac{\kappa}{n}}}\right)^{2}.

The following Energy Lemma follows from the above Monotonicity Lemma in a
standard way, see e.g.
[
SaShUr7
]
. Recall that for a subset
\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{D}
, and for a cube
J\in\mathcal{D}
, there are
projections
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{J}}^{\omega}\equiv\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{J}}\bigtriangleup_{J^{\prime};\kappa}^{\omega}
and
\mathsf{P}_{J}^{\omega}\equiv\sum_{J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}:\ J^{\prime}\subset J}\bigtriangleup_{J^{\prime};\kappa}^{\omega}
. Recall also that
\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\equiv\left\{h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega,a}\right\}_{a\in\Gamma_{J,n,\kappa}}
is the vector of Alpert wavelets
associated with the cube
J
.

Lemma 46

(
Energy Lemma
)
.

Fix
\kappa\geq 1
and a locally finite positive Borel measure
\omega
. Let
J\
be a cube in
\mathcal{D}
. Let
\Psi_{J}\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)
be supported in
J
with vanishing
\omega
-means up to order less than
\kappa
. Let
\nu
be a positive
measure supported in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J
with
\gamma>1
.
Let
T^{\alpha}
be a smooth
\alpha
-fractional singular integral
operator with
0\leq\alpha<n
. Then we have the ‘pivotal’ bound

(3.17)

\left|\left\langle T^{\alpha}\left(\varphi\nu\right),\Psi_{J}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\right|\lesssim C_{\gamma}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\nu\right)\ell\left(J\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\left\|\Psi_{J}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\frac{\left\|\left|\mathbf{h}_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}\right|\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}{\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}}\ ,

for any function
\varphi
with
\left|\varphi\right|\leq 1
.

4.
The strong
\kappa
-pivotal corona decomposition

To set the stage for control of the stopping form below in the absence of
the energy condition, we construct the
strong
\kappa
-pivotal

corona decomposition for
f\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\mu\right)
, in
analogy with the energy version for
L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)
and
L^{2}\left(\omega\right)
used in the two part paper
[
LaSaShUr3
]
,
[
Lac
]
and in
[
SaShUr7
]
.

Fix
\gamma>1
and define
\mathcal{G}_{0}=\left\{F_{1}^{0}\right\}
to
consist of the single cube
F_{1}^{0}
, and define the first generation
\mathcal{G}_{1}=\left\{F_{k}^{1}\right\}_{k}
of
\kappa
-pivotal
stopping children
of
F_{1}^{0}
to be the
maximal
dyadic subcubes
I
of
F_{0}
satisfying

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{F_{1}^{0}}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|I\right|_{\omega}\geq\gamma\left|I\right|_{\sigma}.

Then define the second generation
\mathcal{G}_{2}=\left\{F_{k}^{2}\right\}_{k}
of CZ
\kappa
-pivotal

s
-stopping children of
F_{1}^{0}

to be the
maximal
dyadic subcubes
I
of some
F_{k}^{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
satisfying

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{F_{k}^{1}}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|I\right|_{\omega}\geq\gamma\left|I\right|_{\sigma}.

Continue by recursion to define
\mathcal{G}_{n}
for all
n\geq 0
, and
then set

\mathcal{F\equiv}\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{G}_{n}=\left\{F_{k}^{n}:n\geq 0,k\geq 1\right\}

to be the set of all
\kappa
-pivotal stopping intervals in
F_{1}^{0}

obtained in this way.

4.1.
Carleson condition for stopping cubes and corona controls

The
\varepsilon
-strong
\sigma
-Carleson condition (
2.17
)
holds for a
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-full grid
\mathcal{F}
by the
usual calculation,

\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\left\{\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F^{\prime},\mathbf{1}_{F}\sigma\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\omega}\right\}\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\ .

Indeed, set
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(F\right)
to be the
\ell^{th}
generation of
\mathcal{F}
-subcubes of
F
, and define
\mathcal{F}\left(F\right)=\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\limits_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(F\right)
to be the
collection of all
\mathcal{F}
-subcubes of
F
. Then if
\frac{\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa}+1}{\gamma}<\frac{1}{2}
and
\mathcal{F}
is a
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-full grid, we have the
\varepsilon
-strong
\sigma
-Carleson condition,

(4.1)

\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}\left(F\right)}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}^{\left(\ell\right)}\left(F\right)}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa}+1}{\gamma}\right)^{\ell}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\leq 2\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\ .

Using Lemma
30
, this Carleson condition delivers a basic method
of control by quasiorthogonality (see
[
LaSaShUr3
]
and
[
SaShUr7
]

for the case
s=0
),

(4.2)

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left|F\right|_{\sigma}\left(\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}E_{F}^{\sigma}f\right)^{2}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2},

provided
\mathcal{F}
is a
\beta
-full grid and
\left|s\right|

is sufficiently small depending on
\beta
, which is used repeatedly in
conjunction with orthogonality of Sobolev projections
\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g
,

(4.3)

\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}=\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}.

Recall that for an arbitrary grid
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}
, the
coronas
\mathcal{C}_{F}
for
F\in\mathcal{F}
are defined by

\mathcal{C}_{F}\equiv\left\{I\in\mathcal{D}:I\subset F\text{ and }I\not\subset F^{\prime}\text{ for any }F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}\text{
with }F^{\prime}\varsubsetneqq F\right\}.

We have, from the definition of the stopping times
\mathcal{F}
above, that
the
\left(\beta,\varepsilon\right)
-strong
\kappa
-pivotal control
holds for
\beta>0
sufficiently small, i.e.

(4.4)

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{F}\sigma\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|I\right|_{\omega}<\Gamma\left|I\right|_{\sigma},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{C}_{F}\text{ and }F\in\mathcal{F}.

5.
Reduction of the proof to local forms

To prove Theorem
2
, we begin by proving the bilinear
form bound,

\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\left(\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}+\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\left(\kappa\right)}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{TR}_{\left(T^{\alpha}\right)^{\ast}}^{\left(\kappa\right)}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)+\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa}+\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\kappa,\ast}\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},

for
0<\beta,\varepsilon<1
sufficiently small. Following the weighted
Haar expansions of Nazarov, Treil and Volberg, we write
f
and
g
in
weighted Alpert wavelet expansions,

(5.1)

\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}=\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa_{2}}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}.

Then following
[
SaShUr7
]
and many others, the
L^{2}
inner product in (
5.1
) can be expanded as

\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}=\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa_{2}}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}\ \text{and }J\in\mathcal{D}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa_{2}}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}\ .

Then the sum is further decomposed by first the
Cube Size Splitting
,
then using the
Shifted Corona Decomposition
, according to the
Canonical Splitting
. We assume the reader is familiar with the notation and
arguments in the first eight sections of
[
SaShUr7
]
. The
n
-dimensional
decompositions used in
[
SaShUr7
]
are in spirit the same as the
one-dimensional decompositions in
[
LaSaShUr3
]
, as well as the
n
-dimensional decompositions in
[
LaWi
]
, but differ in significant
details.

A fundamental result of Nazarov, Treil and Volberg
[
NTV4
]
is that all
the cubes
I
and
J
appearing in the bilinear form above may be assumed to
be
\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
, where a dyadic interval
K
is
\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
, or simply
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
, if for
every
dyadic supercube
L
of
K
, it is the case that

either

K
has side length at least
2^{1-r}
times that of
L
,

or

K\Subset_{\left(r,\varepsilon\right)}L
. We say that a
dyadic cube
K
is
\left(r,\varepsilon\right)
-
deeply embedded
in
a dyadic cube
L
, or simply
r
-deeply embedded
in
L
, which we
write as
K\Subset_{r,\varepsilon}L
, when
K\subset L
and both

(5.2)

\displaystyle\ell\left(K\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{-r}\ell\left(L\right),

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(K,\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\limits_{L^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}L}\partial L^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\ell\left(K\right)^{\varepsilon}\ell\left(L\right)^{1-\varepsilon}.

Here is a brief schematic diagram as in
[
AlSaUr
]
, summarizing the
shifted corona decompositions as used in
[
AlSaUr
]
and
[
SaShUr7
]

for Alpert and Haar wavelet expansions of
f
and
g
. We first introduce
parameters as in
[
AlSaUr
]
and
[
SaShUr7
]
. We will choose
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small later in the argument, and then
r
must
be chosen sufficiently large depending on
\varepsilon
in order to reduce
matters to
\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
functions by the
Nazarov, Treil and Volberg argument.

Definition 47
.

The parameters
\tau
and
\rho
are fixed to
satisfy

\tau>r\text{ and }\rho>r+\tau,

where
r
is the goodness parameter already fixed.

\framebox{$\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}&&&&&&\\
\downarrow&&&&&&\\
\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{{}_{\mathbf{\rho}}\Supset}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\cap}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\diagup}\left(f,g\right)\\
\downarrow&&&&&&\\
\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iagonal}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{a}bove}}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isjoint}}\left(f,g\right)\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow&&&&\\
\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)&&\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}^{1}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}^{2}\left(f,g\right)&&\\
\downarrow&&&&&&\\
\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{p}araproduct}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}eighbour}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)&+&\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{c}ommutator}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)\end{array}$}

5.1.
Cube size splitting

The Nazarov, Treil and Volberg
Cube Size Splitting
of the inner
product
\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}

splits the pairs of cubes
\left(I,J\right)
in a simultaneous Alpert
decomposition of
f
and
g
into four groups determined by relative
position, is given by

\displaystyle\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f,g\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{I,J\in\mathcal{D}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\Subset_{\rho,\varepsilon}I\end{subarray}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}+\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J_{\rho,\varepsilon}\Supset I\end{subarray}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle+\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\cap I=\emptyset\text{
and }\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\notin\left[2^{-\rho},2^{\rho}\right]\end{subarray}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle+\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
2^{-\rho}\leq\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\leq 2^{\rho}\end{subarray}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\rho,\varepsilon}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{{}_{\rho,\varepsilon}\Supset}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\cap}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\diagup}\left(f,g\right).

Note however that the assumption the cubes
I
and
J
are
\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
remains in force throughout the proof.

We will now make use of the
\kappa
-cube testing and triple testing
constants, defined in (
3.6
) and (
3.7
), to prove the following bound in the Sobolev setting, which in the case
s=0
was proved in
[
Saw6
, see Lemma 31]
following the Nazarov, Treil
and Volberg arguments for Haar wavelets in
[
SaShUr7
, see the proof of Lemma 7.1]
(see also
[
LaSaShUr3
]
),

(5.3)

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\cap}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\diagup}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq C\left(\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}+\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{\kappa,-s}+\mathcal{WBP}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\left(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\right),s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},

where if
\Omega_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}
is the set of all dyadic grids,

\mathcal{WBP}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\left(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\right),s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\equiv\sup_{\mathcal{D}\in\Omega}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}\\
Q\subset 3Q^{\prime}\setminus Q^{\prime}\text{ or }Q^{\prime}\subset 3Q\setminus Q\end{subarray}}\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)^{s}\ell\left(Q^{\prime}\right)^{-s}}{\sqrt{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}\left|Q^{\prime}\right|_{\omega}}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\left(\mathcal{P}_{Q}^{\kappa_{1}}\right)_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}orm}}\left(\sigma\right)\\
g\in\left(\mathcal{P}_{Q^{\prime}}^{\kappa_{2}}\right)_{\mathop{\mathrm{n}orm}}\left(\omega\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\int_{Q^{\prime}}T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{Q}f\right)\ gd\omega\right|<\infty

is a weak boundedness constant that in the case
s=0
was introduced in
[
Saw6
]
. Here we will use the case
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=\kappa
.
However, we only use that this constant is removed in the final section
below using the following bound proved in
[
Saw6
, see (6.25) in Subsection 6.7
and note that only triple testing is needed there by choosing
\ell\left(Q^{\prime}\right)\leq\ell\left(Q\right)
(using duality and
T^{\alpha,\ast}
if needed)]
, and which holds also in the Sobolev setting
using Cauchy-Schwarz and triple testing,

(5.4)

\mathcal{WBP}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\left(\kappa,\kappa\right),s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\leq C_{\kappa}\left(\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{\kappa,-s}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\right).

In fact the stronger bound with absolute values inside the sums in (
5.3
) was proved in the case
s=0
in the previous references,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
J\cap I=\emptyset\text{
and }\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\notin\left[2^{-\rho},2^{\rho}\right]\end{subarray}}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|+\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
2^{-\rho}\leq\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\leq 2^{\rho}\end{subarray}}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}+\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{\kappa,-s}+\mathcal{WBP}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\left(\kappa,\kappa\right),s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}.

This bound will be useful later since it yields the same bound for the sum
of any subcollection of the index set, and for the convenience of the
reader, we prove (
5.5
) below. Since the
below
and
above
forms
\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho},\varepsilon}}\left(f,g\right),\mathsf{B}_{{}_{\mathbf{\rho},\varepsilon}\Supset}\left(f,g\right)
are symmetric, matters are then reduced to proving

(5.6)

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho},\varepsilon}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{s}+\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{-s}+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

We introduce some notation in order to prove (
5.5
). For weighted
Alpert wavelet projections
\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}
, we write
the projection
\mathbb{E}_{I^{\prime};\kappa}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f
onto the child
I^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}\left(I\right)
as
M_{I^{\prime};\kappa}^{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\pm}}
, where
M_{I^{\prime};\kappa}^{\sigma}
is a polynomial of
degree less than
\kappa
restricted to
I^{\prime}
. Then we let
P_{I^{\prime};\kappa}^{\sigma}\equiv\frac{M_{I^{\prime};\kappa}^{\sigma}}{\left\|M_{I^{\prime};\kappa}^{\sigma}\right\|_{\infty}}
be its normalization on
I^{\prime}
. From (
2.9
)
we have the estimate,

(5.7)

\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I^{\prime};\kappa}^{\sigma}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\leq C\frac{\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}}{\sqrt{\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}}}\approx C\frac{\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}}}.

Proof of (
5.5
).

To handle the second term in (
5.5
) we first decompose it into

\displaystyle\left\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}:\ J\subset 3I\\
2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset 3J\\
2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\\
J\not\subset 3I\text{ and }I\not\subset 3J\end{subarray}}\right\}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \equiv A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}.

The proof of the bound for term
A_{3}
is similar to that of the bound for
the first term in (
5.5
), and so we will defer its proof until
after the second term has been proved.

We now consider term
A_{1}
as term
A_{2}
is symmetric. To handle this
term we will write the Alpert functions
h_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}
and
h_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}
as linear combinations of polynomials times
indicators of the children of their supporting cubes, denoted
I_{\theta}

and
J_{\theta^{\prime}}
respectively. Then we use the testing condition
on
I_{\theta}
and
J_{\theta^{\prime}}
when they
overlap
, i.e.
their interiors intersect; we use the weak boundedness property on
I_{\theta}
and
J_{\theta^{\prime}}
when they
touch
, i.e. their
interiors are disjoint but their closures intersect (even in just a point);
and finally we use the
A_{2}^{\alpha}
condition when
I_{\theta}
and
J_{\theta^{\prime}}
are
separated
, i.e. their closures are
disjoint. We will suppose initially that the side length of
J
is at most
the side length
I
, i.e.
\ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)
,
the proof for
J=\pi I
being similar but for one point mentioned below.

So suppose that
I_{\theta}
is a child of
I
and that
J_{\theta^{\prime}}
is a child of
J
. If
J_{\theta^{\prime}}\subset I_{\theta}
we have
using (
5.7
),

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathbf{1}_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\frac{\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(P_{I_{\theta};\kappa}^{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}}\right),P_{J_{\theta^{\prime}};\kappa}^{\omega}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\frac{\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}}\left(\int_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\left|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(P_{I_{\theta};\kappa}^{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}}\right)\right|^{2}d\omega\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|\lesssim\frac{\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}}\mathfrak{T}_{T_{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}\left|I\right|_{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{T_{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|,

where
\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)
denotes the vector Alpert
coefficient of
f
at the dyadic cube
I
. The point referred to above is
that when
J=\pi I
we write

\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(P_{I_{\theta};\kappa}^{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}}\right),P_{J_{\theta^{\prime}};\kappa}^{\omega}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{\omega}=\left\langle P_{I_{\theta};\kappa}^{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}},T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\left(P_{J_{\theta^{\prime}};\kappa}^{\omega}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\right)\right\rangle_{\sigma}\ ,

and get the dual testing constant
\mathfrak{T}_{T_{\alpha}^{\ast}}^{\kappa,s}
. If
J_{\theta^{\prime}}
and
I_{\theta}
touch, then
\ell\left(J_{\theta^{\prime}}\right)\leq\ell\left(I_{\theta}\right)
and we have
J_{\theta^{\prime}}\subset 3I_{\theta}\setminus I_{\theta}
, and so

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathbf{1}_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\frac{\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(P_{I_{\theta};\kappa}^{\sigma}\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}}\right),P_{J_{\theta^{\prime}};\kappa}^{\omega}\mathbf{1}_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\frac{\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}}\mathcal{WBP}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\left(\kappa,\kappa\right),s}\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\frac{\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}=\mathcal{WBP}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\left(\kappa,\kappa\right),s}\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|.

Finally, if
J_{\theta^{\prime}}
and
I_{\theta}
are separated, and if
K
is the smallest (not necessarily dyadic) cube containing both
J_{\theta^{\prime}}
and
I_{\theta}
, then
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I_{\theta},J_{\theta^{\prime}}\right)\approx\ell\left(K\right)
and we have

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathbf{1}_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\frac{\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{1}_{I_{\theta}}\right),\mathbf{1}_{J_{\theta^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\frac{\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}}\frac{1}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I_{\theta},J_{\theta^{\prime}}\right)^{n-\alpha}}\left|I_{\theta}\right|_{\sigma}\left|J_{\theta^{\prime}}\right|_{\omega}\frac{\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|}{\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}}=\frac{\sqrt{\left|I_{\theta}\right|_{\sigma}\left|J_{\theta^{\prime}}\right|_{\omega}}}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I_{\theta},J_{\theta^{\prime}}\right)^{n-\alpha}}\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|\lesssim\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|.

Now we sum over all the children of
J
and
I
satisfying
2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)
for which
J\subset 3I
to obtain that

A_{1}\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}+\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{\kappa,-s}+\mathcal{WBP}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\left(\kappa,\kappa\right),s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}:\ J\subset 3I\\
2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|\ .

It is at this point that the Sobolev norms make their appearance, through an
application of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}:\ J\subset 3I\\
2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|\leq C_{\rho}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}:\ J\subset 3I\\
2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{f_{\kappa}}\left(I\right)\right|^{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{-2s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}:\ J\subset 3I\\
2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\leq\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\widehat{g_{\kappa}}\left(J\right)\right|^{2}\ell\left(J\right)^{2s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

This completes our proof of the bound for the second term in (
5.5
), save for the deferral of term
A_{3}
, which we bound below.

Now we turn to the sum of separated cubes in (
5.5
). We split the
pairs
\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}^{\sigma}\times\mathcal{D}^{\omega}
occurring in the first term in (
5.5
) into two groups, those
with side length of
J
smaller than side length of
I
, and those with side
length of
I
smaller than side length of
J
, treating only the former
case, the latter being symmetric. Thus we prove the following bound:

\mathcal{A}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I,J\in\mathcal{D}\\
I\cap J=\emptyset\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right)\end{subarray}}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}.

We apply the ‘pivotal’ bound (
3.14
) from the Energy Lemma to
estimate the inner product
\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}
and obtain,

\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\ell\left(J\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\,.

Denote by
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}
the
\ell^{\infty}
distance in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
:
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(x,y\right)=\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\left|x_{j}-y_{j}\right|
. We now estimate separately the long-range and
mid-range cases where
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)\geq\ell\left(I\right)
holds or not, and we decompose
\mathcal{A}
accordingly:

\mathcal{A}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{m}id}}\left(f,g\right).

The long-range case
: We begin with the case where
\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(J,I\right)
is at least
\ell\left(I\right)
, i.e.
J\cap 3I=\emptyset
. Since
J
and
I
are separated by at least
\max\left\{\ell\left(J\right),\ell\left(I\right)\right\}
, we have the inequality

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\approx\int_{I}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|y-c_{J}\right|^{n+1-\alpha}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\left(y\right)\right|d\sigma\left(y\right)\lesssim\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\ell\left(I\right)^{s}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)^{n+1-\alpha}},

since
\int_{I}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\left(y\right)\right|d\sigma\left(y\right)\leq\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}
and
\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}=\ell\left(I\right)^{-s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\sigma\right)}
. Thus with
A\left(f,g\right)=\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)
we have

\displaystyle A\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{J\;:\;\ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right):\ \mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\geq\ell\left(I\right)}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}\right)^{s}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)^{n+1-\alpha}}\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}A\left(I,J\right);

\displaystyle\text{with }A\left(I,J\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\ell\left(I\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}\right)^{s}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)^{n+1-\alpha}}\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}};

\displaystyle\text{ and }\mathcal{P}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}:\ell\left(J\right)\leq\ell\left(I\right)\text{ and
}\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\geq\ell\left(I\right)\right\}.

Now let
\mathcal{D}_{N}\equiv\left\{K\in\mathcal{D}:\ell\left(K\right)=2^{N}\right\}
for each
N\in\mathbb{Z}
. For
N\in\mathbb{Z}
and
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, we further decompose
A\left(f,g\right)
by pigeonholing
the sidelengths of
I
and
J
by
2^{N}
and
2^{N-t}
respectively:

\displaystyle A\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{N}^{t}\left(f,g\right);

\displaystyle A_{N}^{t}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N}^{t}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}A\left(I,J\right)

\displaystyle\text{where }\mathcal{P}_{N}^{t}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}_{N}\times\mathcal{D}_{N-t}:\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\geq\ell\left(I\right)\right\}.

Now
A_{N}^{t}\left(f,g\right)=A_{N}^{t}\left(\mathsf{P}_{N;\kappa}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{N-t;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)
where
\mathsf{P}_{M;\kappa}^{\mu}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{M}}\bigtriangleup_{K;\kappa}^{\mu}
denotes Alpert projection onto the linear span
\mathop{\mathrm{S}pan}\left\{h_{K;\kappa}^{\mu,a}\right\}_{K\in\mathcal{D}_{M},a\in\Gamma_{K,n,\kappa}}
, and so by orthogonality of the
projections
\left\{\mathsf{P}_{M;\kappa}^{\mu}\right\}_{M\in\mathbb{Z}}
we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{N}^{t}\left(f,g\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\left|A_{N}^{t}\left(\mathsf{P}_{N;\kappa}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{N-t;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right|\leq\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\|A_{N}^{t}\right\|\left\|\mathsf{P}_{N;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{N-t;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\|A_{N}^{t}\right\|\right\}\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{N;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{N-t;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\sup_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\|A_{N}^{t}\right\|\right\}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}.

Thus it suffices to show an estimate uniform in
N
with geometric decay in
t
, and we will show

(5.8)

\left|A_{N}^{t}\left(f,g\right)\right|\leq C2^{-t}\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }t\geq 0\text{ and }N\in\mathbb{Z}.

We now pigeonhole the distance between
I
and
J
:

\displaystyle A_{N}^{t}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\ell=0}^{\infty}A_{N,\ell}^{t}\left(f,g\right);

\displaystyle A_{N,\ell}^{t}\left(f,g\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,\ell}^{t}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}A\left(I,J\right)

\displaystyle\text{where }\mathcal{P}_{N,\ell}^{t}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}_{N}\times\mathcal{D}_{N-s}:\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\approx 2^{N+\ell}\right\}.

If we define
\mathcal{H}\left(A_{N,\ell}^{t}\right)
to be the bilinear
form on
\ell^{2}\times\ell^{2}
with matrix
\left[A\left(I,J\right)\right]_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,\ell}^{t}}
, then it remains
to show that the norm
\left\|\mathcal{H}\left(A_{N,\ell}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\ell^{2}\rightarrow\ell^{2}}
is bounded by
C2^{-t\left(1-s\right)-\ell}\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}
. In turn, this is equivalent to
showing that the norm
\left\|\mathcal{H}\left(B_{N,\ell}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\ell^{2}\rightarrow\ell^{2}}
of the bilinear form
\mathcal{H}\left(B_{N,\ell}^{t}\right)\equiv\mathcal{H}\left(A_{N,\ell}^{t}\right)^{\mathop{\mathrm{t}r}}\mathcal{H}\left(A_{N,\ell}^{t}\right)
on
the sequence space
\ell^{2}
is bounded by
C^{2}2^{-2t\left(1-s\right)-2\ell}A_{2}^{\alpha}
. Here
\mathcal{H}\left(B_{N,\ell}^{t}\right)

is the quadratic form with matrix kernel
\left[B_{N,\ell}^{t}\left(J,J^{\prime}\right)\right]_{J,J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{N-s}}
having
entries:

B_{N,\ell}^{t}\left(J,J^{\prime}\right)\equiv\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}:\ \mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\approx\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J^{\prime}\right)\approx 2^{N+\ell}}A\left(I,J\right)A\left(I,J^{\prime}\right),\ \ \ \ \ \text{for }J,J^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{N-t}.

We are reduced to showing,

\left\|\mathcal{H}\left(B_{N,\ell}^{t}\right)\right\|_{\ell^{2}\rightarrow\ell^{2}}\leq C2^{-2t\left(1-s\right)-2\ell}A_{2}^{\alpha}\ \ \ \ \text{for }t\geq 0\text{, }\ell\geq 0\text{ and }N\in\mathbb{Z},

which is an estimate in which Alpert projections no longer play a role, and
this estimate is proved as in
[
NTV4
]
, and more precisely as in
[
SaShUr7
]
. Note that the only arithmetic difference in the argument here is
that in the estimates, the parameter
t>0
is replaced by
t\left(1-s\right)>0
, which has no effect on the conclusion. This completes our
proof of the long-range estimate

\mathcal{A}^{\mathop{\mathrm{l}ong}}\left(f,g\right)\lesssim\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

At this point we pause to complete the bound for
A_{3}
in the second term
in (
5.5
). Indeed, the deferred term
A_{3}
can be handled using
the above argument since
3J\cap I=\emptyset=J\cap 3I
implies that we can
use the Energy Lemma as we did above.

The mid range case
: Let

\mathcal{P}\equiv\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}:J\text{ is good},\ \ell\left(J\right)\leq 2^{-\rho}\ell\left(I\right),\text{ }J\subset 3I\setminus I\right\}.

For
\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}
, the ‘pivotal’ bound (
3.14
) from the Energy Lemma gives

\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\ell\left(J\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\,.

Now we pigeonhole the lengths of
I
and
J
and the distance between them
by defining

\mathcal{P}_{N,d}^{t}\equiv\left\{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{D}\times\mathcal{D}:J\text{ is good},\ \ell\left(I\right)=2^{N},\ \ell\left(J\right)=2^{N-t},\text{ }J\subset 3I\setminus I,\ 2^{d-1}\leq\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\leq 2^{d}\right\}.

Note that the closest a good cube
J
can come to
I
is determined by the
goodness inequality, which gives this bound for
2^{d}\geq\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)
:

\displaystyle 2^{d}\geq\frac{1}{2}\ell\left(I\right)^{1-\varepsilon}\ell\left(J\right)^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}2^{N\left(1-\varepsilon\right)}2^{\left(N-t\right)\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{2}2^{N-\varepsilon t};

\displaystyle\text{which implies }N-\varepsilon t-1\leq d\leq N,

where the last inequality holds because we are in the case of the mid-range
term. Thus we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\ell\left(J\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ =\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{t=\rho}^{\infty}\ \sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\ \sum_{d=N-\varepsilon t-1}^{N}\ \sum_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}_{N,d}^{t}}\ \left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\ell\left(J\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}.

Now we use

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{I}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left(\ell\left(J\right)+\left|y-c_{J}\right|\right)^{n+1-\alpha}}\left|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\left(y\right)\right|d\sigma\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{2^{N-t}}{2^{d\left(n+1-\alpha\right)}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\ell\left(I\right)^{s}\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}

and apply Cauchy-Schwarz in
J
and use
J\subset 3I\setminus I
to get

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{P}}\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{t=\rho}^{\infty}\ \sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\ \sum_{d=N-\varepsilon t-1}^{N}\ \sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\frac{2^{N-t\left(1-s\right)}2^{N\left(n-\alpha\right)}}{2^{d\left(n+1-\alpha\right)}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\frac{\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\sqrt{\left|3I\setminus I\right|_{\omega}}}{2^{N\left(n-\alpha\right)}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-t}\\
J\subset 3I\setminus I\text{ and
}\mathop{\mathrm{d}ist}\left(I,J\right)\approx 2^{d}\end{subarray}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(1+\varepsilon t\right)\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{t=\rho}^{\infty}\ \sum_{N\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{2^{N-t\left(1-s\right)}2^{N\left(n-\alpha\right)}}{2^{\left(N-\varepsilon t\right)\left(n+1-\alpha\right)}}\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\sqrt{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\in\mathcal{D}_{N-t}\\
J\subset 3I\setminus I\end{subarray}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(1+\varepsilon t\right)\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{t=\rho}^{\infty}2^{-t\left[1-s-\varepsilon\left(n+1-\alpha\right)\right]}\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\lesssim\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},

where in the third line above we have used
\sum_{d=N-\varepsilon t-1}^{N}1\lesssim 1+\varepsilon t
, and in the last line

\frac{2^{N-t\left(1-s\right)}2^{N\left(n-\alpha\right)}}{2^{\left(N-\varepsilon t\right)\left(n+1-\alpha\right)}}=2^{-t\left[1-s-\varepsilon\left(n+1-\alpha\right)\right]},

followed by Cauchy-Schwarz in
I
and
N
, using that we have bounded
overlap in the triples of
I
for
I\in\mathcal{D}_{N}
. We have also
assumed here that
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1-s}{n+1-\alpha}
, and this
completes the proof of (
5.5
).

5.2.
Shifted corona decomposition

To prove (
5.6
), we recall the
Shifted Corona
Decomposition
, as opposed to the
parallel
corona decomposition used
in
[
Saw6
]
, associated with the Calderón-Zygmund
\kappa
-pivotal
stopping cubes
\mathcal{F}
introduced above. But first we must invoke
standard arguments, using the full
\kappa
-cube testing conditions (
3.7
), to permit us to assume that
f
and
g
are supported in a
finite union of dyadic cubes
F_{0}
on which they have vanishing moments of
order less than
\kappa
.

5.2.1.
The initial reduction using full testing

For this construction, we will follow the treatment as given in
[
SaShUr12
]
. We first restrict
f
and
g
to be supported in a large common
cube
Q_{\infty}
. Then we cover
Q_{\infty}
with
2^{n}
pairwise
disjoint cubes
I_{\infty}\in\mathcal{D}
with
\ell\left(I_{\infty}\right)=\ell\left(Q_{\infty}\right)
. We now claim we can reduce
matters to consideration of the
2^{2n}
forms

\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset I_{\infty}}\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}:\ J\subset J_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}gd\omega,

as both
I_{\infty}
and
J_{\infty}
range over the dyadic cubes as
above. First we note that when
I_{\infty}
and
J_{\infty}
are distinct,
the corresponding form is included in the sum
\mathsf{B}_{\cap}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{B}_{\diagup}\left(f,g\right)
, and hence controlled.
Thus it remains to consider the forms with
I_{\infty}=J_{\infty}
and use
the cubes
I_{\infty}
as the starting cubes in our corona construction
below. Indeed, we have from (
2.6
) that

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset I_{\infty}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f+\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\sigma}f,

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}:\ J\subset I_{\infty}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g+\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}g,

which can then be used to write the bilinear form
\int\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right)gd\omega
as a sum of the forms

(5.9)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f\right)gd\omega=\sum_{I_{\infty}}\left\{\sum_{I,J\in\mathcal{D}:\ I,J\subset I_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}gd\omega\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset I_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right)\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}gd\omega+\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}:\ J\subset I_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right)\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}gd\omega+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right)\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}gd\omega\right\},

taken over the
2^{n}
cubes
I_{\infty}
above.

The second, third and fourth sums in (
5.9
) can be controlled
by the full testing conditions (
3.7
), e.g.

(5.10)

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset I_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right)\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}gd\omega\right|=\left|\int_{I_{\infty}}\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset I_{\infty}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right)T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\left(\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)d\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset I_{\infty}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I_{\infty}}T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\left(\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{I\in\mathcal{D}:\ I\subset I_{\infty}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{I_{\infty}}T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\left(\frac{\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}g}{\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I_{\infty};\kappa}^{\omega}g\right\|_{L^{\infty}}}\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}}^{\kappa}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ ,

and similarly for the third and fourth sum.

5.2.2.
The corona and shifted corona projections

Given a grid
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{D}
and its associated coronas
\mathcal{C}_{F}
, define the two Alpert corona projections,

\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}\equiv\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{F}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}\text{ and }\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}\equiv\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa_{2}}^{\omega}\ ,

where

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{C}_{F}\setminus\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\tau}\left(F\right)\right]\cup\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\limits_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F\right)}\left[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\tau}\left(F^{\prime}\right)\setminus\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\tau}\left(F\right)\right];

\displaystyle\text{where }\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\tau}\left(F\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left\{J\in\mathcal{D}:J\subset F\text{ and }\ell\left(J\right)>2^{-\tau}\ell\left(F\right)\right\}.

Thus the
shifted
corona
\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}
has
the top
\tau
levels from
\mathcal{C}_{F}
removed, and includes the
first
\tau
levels from each of its
\mathcal{F}
-children, except if they
have already been removed. We must restrict the Alpert supports of
f
and
g
to
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
cubes, as defined e.g. in
[
Saw6
]
, so that with
the superscript
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
denoting this restriction,

\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f=\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}\text{ and }\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g=\sum_{J\in\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood},\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa_{2}}^{\omega}\ ,

where
\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{F}\cap\mathcal{D}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood},\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}\equiv\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}\cap\mathcal{D}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
, and
\mathcal{D}^{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}
consists of the
\left(r,\varepsilon\right)-\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
cubes in
\mathcal{D}
.

A simple but important property is the fact that the
\tau
-shifted coronas

\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}
have overlap bounded by
\tau
:

(5.12)

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}\left(J\right)\leq\tau,\ \ \ \ \ J\in\mathcal{D}.

It is convenient, for use in the canonical splitting below, to introduce the
following shorthand notation for
F,G\in\mathcal{F}
:

\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{G}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}^{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}\equiv\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\mathcal{C}_{F}\text{ and }J\in\mathcal{C}_{G}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}\\
J\Subset_{\rho}I\end{subarray}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right),\left(\bigtriangleup_{J;\kappa}^{\omega}g\right)\right\rangle_{\omega}\ .

5.3.
Canonical splitting

We then proceed with the
Canonical Splitting
as in
[
SaShUr7
]
,
but with Alpert wavelets in place of Haar wavelets,

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\rho}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F,G\in\mathcal{F}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{G}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}^{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}^{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F,G\in\mathcal{F}\\
G\subsetneqq F\end{subarray}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{G}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}^{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F,G\in\mathcal{F}\\
G\supsetneqq F\end{subarray}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{G}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}^{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F,G\in\mathcal{F}\\
F\cap G=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{G}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}^{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iagonal}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{a}bove}}\left(f,g\right)+\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isjoint}}\left(f,g\right).

The two forms
\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{a}bove}}\left(f,g\right)

and
\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}isjoint}}\left(f,g\right)
each vanish just as
in
[
SaShUr7
]
, since there are no pairs
\left(I,J\right)\in\mathcal{C}_{F}\times\mathcal{C}_{G}^{\mathbf{\tau}-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}
with both (
i
)
J\Subset_{\rho}I
and (
ii
) either
F\subsetneqq G

or
G\cap F=\emptyset
.

5.3.1.
The far below form

Here is a generalization to weighted Sobolev spaces of the Intertwining
Proposition from
[
Saw6
, Proposition 36 on page 35]
, that uses strong
\kappa
-pivotal conditions with Alpert wavelets. Recall that
0<\varepsilon<1
and
r
is chosen sufficiently large depending on
\varepsilon
. The
argument given here is considerably simpler than that in
[
Saw6
]
.

Proposition 48

(The Intertwining Proposition)
.

Suppose
\sigma,\omega
are positive locally finite Borel
measures on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, that
\sigma
is doubling, and that
\mathcal{F}
satisfies an
\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}
-strong
\sigma
-Carleson
condition. Then for a smooth
\alpha
-fractional singular integral
T^{\alpha}
, and for
\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}
functions
f\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
and
g\in W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)
, and with
\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\geq 1
sufficiently large, we
have the following bound for
\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\ \left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\mathbf{\tau}-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}
:

(5.13)

\left|\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{f}ar}\mathop{\mathrm{b}elow}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa_{1}}+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\ \left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},

where
\beta
is as in Lemma
39
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
is
sufficiently small.

Proof.

We write

\displaystyle f_{F}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sum_{I:\ I\supsetneqq F}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\bigtriangleup_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I\right)}\left(\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f-\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I\right)}\left(\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right)-\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\beta_{F}-\gamma_{F}\ ,

and then

\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}f_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\beta_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}+\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\ \left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\gamma_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}\ .

Now we use the pivotal bound (
3.14
),

\left|\left\langle T^{\alpha}\left(\varphi\nu\right),\Psi_{J}\right\rangle_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}\right|\lesssim\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(J,\nu\right)\ell\left(J\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\left\|\Psi_{J}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},

the pivotal stopping control (
4.4
),

\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(I,\mathbf{1}_{F}\sigma\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\left|I\right|_{\omega}<\Gamma\left|I\right|_{\sigma},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{C}_{F}\text{ and }F\in\mathcal{F},

and (
3.15
), namely

\mathrm{P}_{k}^{\alpha}\left(J,\sigma\mathbf{1}_{K\setminus I}\right)\lesssim\left(\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\ell\left(I\right)}\right)^{k-\varepsilon\left(n+k-\alpha\right)}\mathrm{P}_{k}^{\alpha}\left(I,\sigma\mathbf{1}_{K\setminus I}\right),

to obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\gamma_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\sigma\right)\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)}\right)^{\kappa-\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F,\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\sigma\right)\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

equals

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)}\right)^{\kappa-\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)-s}\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)^{-s}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\left\{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F,\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\sigma\right)\sqrt{\left|\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right|_{\omega}}\right\}\sqrt{\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right|_{\omega}}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)}\right)^{\eta+\eta^{\prime}}\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)^{-s}\left\{\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\ \sqrt{\left|\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right|_{\sigma}}\right\}\sqrt{\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right|_{\omega}}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},

where we have used the pivotal stopping inequality, and written

\kappa-\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)-s+\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}=\eta+\eta^{\prime},

with
\eta,\eta^{\prime}>0
to be chosen later. Note that this requires
the Alpert parameter
\kappa
to satisfy

(5.14)

\kappa>\frac{\varepsilon\left(n-\alpha\right)+s-\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}{1-\varepsilon}.

Then by Cauchy-Schwarz we have

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\gamma_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)^{-2s}\left|\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right|_{\sigma}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)}\right)^{2\eta}\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right|_{\omega}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\left(\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)}\right)^{2\eta^{\prime}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The square of the first factor satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right|_{\sigma}\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)^{-2s}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)}\right)^{2\eta}\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right|_{\omega}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime};\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F\in\mathcal{F}\\
F\subset F^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)}\right)^{2\eta}\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}F^{\prime};\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}=\sum_{F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F^{\prime\prime};\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{F^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{F}}\left(F^{\prime\prime}\right)}\ell\left(F^{\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|F^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F^{\prime\prime};\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\ell\left(F^{\prime\prime}\right)^{-2s}\left|F^{\prime\prime}\right|_{\sigma}\lesssim\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}\ ,

where the first inequality in the last line follows from the strong
\sigma
-Carleson condition, and the second inequality follows from the
Quasiorthogonality Lemma
30
since Lemma
39
shows that
\mathcal{F}
is a
\left(\beta,\sigma\right)
-full grid for a
sufficiently small
\beta>0
. The square of the second factor satisfies

\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\right)}\right)^{2\eta^{\prime}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\lesssim\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\leq\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\ ,

where we have used
\eta^{\prime}>0
.

It remains to bound
\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\beta_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}
where
\beta_{F}=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I\right)}\left(\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right)
. The difference
between the previous estimate and this one is that the averages
\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\left|\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right|

inside the Poisson kernel have been replaced with the sum of averages
\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I\right)}\left|\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right|
, but where the sum is
taken over pairwise disjoint sets
\left\{\theta\left(I\right)\right\}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}
.
We start with

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\beta_{F},g_{F}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|\lesssim\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I\right)}\left|\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right|\sigma\right)\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I\right)}\sigma\right)\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\equiv S.

Then we use

\displaystyle\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I\right)}\sigma\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sup_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\right)\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\sum_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\mathbf{1}_{\theta\left(I\right)}\sigma\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sup_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\right)\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\sigma\right),

and obtain that

S\leq\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left(\sup_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\right)\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\sigma\right)\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

Now we define
G_{m}\left[F\right]\in\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F,\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\right]
so that
\sup_{I:\ \pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F\subsetneqq I\subset\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{I;\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}=\left\|\mathbb{E}_{G_{m}\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}
, and
dominate
S
by

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{G_{m}\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(F,\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\sigma\right)\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{G_{m}\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{\eta}\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(G_{m}\left[F\right],\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\sigma\right)\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{G_{m}\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{\eta}\left\{\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(G_{m}\left[F\right],\mathbf{1}_{\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\setminus\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F}\sigma\right)\left(\frac{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\left|G_{m}\left[F\right]\right|_{\omega}}\right\}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times\left(\frac{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{-\varepsilon}\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|G_{m}\left[F\right]\right|_{\omega}}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},

and then continue with

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{G_{m}\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(G\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{\eta}\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\left\{\sqrt{\left|G_{m}\left[F\right]\right|_{\sigma}}\right\}\left(\frac{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{-\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|G_{m}\left[F\right]\right|_{\omega}}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{G\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)^{-s}\sqrt{\left|G_{m}\left[F\right]\right|_{\sigma}}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{\eta-s}\left(\frac{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{-\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|G_{m}\left[F\right]\right|_{\omega}}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{G_{m}\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{\eta-s}\left(\frac{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{-\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|G_{m}\left[F\right]\right|_{\omega}}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}.

Since there is geometric gain in the product

\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{\eta-s}\left(\frac{\ell\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{-\varepsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\left|F\right|_{\omega}}{\left|G_{m}\left[F\right]\right|_{\omega}}}\leq\left(\frac{\ell\left(F\right)}{\ell\left(G_{m}\left[F\right]\right)}\right)^{\eta-s}\lesssim 2^{-m\left(\eta-s\right)},

provided
\eta>s
, an application of Cauchy-Schwarz finishes the proof
since
G_{m}\left[F\right]
is uniquely determined by
F
and
m
in the
tower
\left(\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m}F,\pi_{\mathcal{F}}^{m+1}F\right]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{-m\left(\eta-s\right)}\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{G_{m}\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{-m\left(\eta-s\right)}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{G_{m}\left[F\right];\kappa_{1}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\left\|g_{F}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}2^{-m\left(\eta-s\right)}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}=C\mathcal{V}_{2,\beta,\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{\alpha,\kappa}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

Note that we have used
\eta>s
, which requires a bit more on
\kappa

than was used in (
5.14
), namely that

\kappa-\varepsilon\left(n+\kappa-\alpha\right)-s+\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}=\eta+\eta^{\prime}>s,

which requires

(5.15)

\kappa>\frac{\varepsilon\left(n-\alpha\right)+2s-\theta_{\sigma}^{\mathop{\mathrm{r}ev}}}{1-\varepsilon}.

5.3.2.
The diagonal form

To handle the diagonal term
\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iagonal}}\left(f,g\right)
, we decompose according to the stopping times
\mathcal{F}
,
which we recall are
\beta
-full for some
\beta>0
by Lemma
39
,

\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iagonal}}\left(f,g\right)=\sum_{F\in\mathcal{F}}\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)\equiv\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left(\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right),\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\rangle_{\omega}^{\Subset_{\mathbf{\rho}}},

and it is enough, using Cauchy-Schwarz and quasiorthogonality (
4.2
) in
f
, together with orthogonality (
4.3
) in both
f
and
g
,
to prove the ‘below form’ bound involving the usual cube testing constant,

(5.16)

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\rho}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{s}+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\ \left(\ell\left(F\right)^{-s}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{F;\kappa}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\left|F\right|_{\sigma}}+\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\right)\ \left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}\ .

Indeed, using quasiorthogonality, Lemma
30
, and orthogonality of
projections
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f
and
\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g
this then gives the
estimate,

(5.17)

\left|\mathsf{T}_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}iagonal}}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\left(\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{s}+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}}\right)\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}.

Thus at this point we have essentially reduced the proof of Theorem
2
to

(1)

proving (
5.16
),

(2)

and controlling the triple polynomial testing condition (
3.7
) by the usual cube testing condition and the classical Muckenhoupt
condition.

In the next section we address the first issue by proving the inequality (
5.16
) for the below forms
\mathsf{B}_{\Subset_{\rho}}^{F}\left(f,g\right)
. In the final section, we address the second issue
and complete the proofs of our theorems by drawing together all of the
estimates.

6.
The Nazarov, Treil and Volberg reach for Alpert wavelets

It will be convenient to denote our fractional singular integral operators
by
T^{\lambda}
,
0\leq\lambda<n
, instead of
T^{\alpha}
, thus
freeing up
\alpha
for the familiar role of denoting multi-indices in
\mathbb{Z}_{+}^{n}
. Before getting started, we note that for a doubling
measure
\mu
, a cube
I
and a polynomial
P
, we have

\left\|P\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mu\right)}=\sup_{x\in I}\left|P\left(x\right)\right|

because
\mu
charges all open sets, and so in particular,
\left\|P\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\sigma\right)}=\left\|P\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\omega\right)}
.

We will often follow the analogous arguments in
[
AlSaUr
]
, and point out
the places where significant new approaches are needed. See
[
AlSaUr
]

for a review of the classical reach of Nazarov, Treil and Volberg using Haar
wavelet projections
\bigtriangleup_{I}^{\sigma}
, namely the beautiful
and ingenious ‘thinking outside the box’ idea of the paraproduct / stopping
