\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2,\varepsilon}^{\lambda,1}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},

if we take
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1+s}{n+1-\lambda}
.

Finally using orthogonality of both
\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}}^{\sigma}f\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}
and
\left\{\mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{C}_{F}^{\tau-\mathop{\mathrm{s}hift}}}^{\omega}\right\}_{F\in\mathcal{F}}
, we obtain

(6.15)

\left|\mathsf{B}_{\mathop{\mathrm{s}top};\kappa}\left(f,g\right)\right|\lesssim\sqrt{A_{2}^{\lambda}}\left\|f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)},\ \ \ \ \ \left|s\right|<1.

7.
Conclusion of the proofs

Collecting all the estimates proved above, namely (
5.3
), (
5.10
), (
5.4
), (
5.13
), Lemma
40
,
(
5.17
), (
6.5
), (
6.12
), (
6.13
) and (
6.15
), we obtain just as in
[
AlSaUr
]
that for any dyadic grid

\mathcal{D}
, and any admissible truncation of
T^{\alpha}
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathsf{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathcal{D}}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathcal{D}}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{\kappa,-s}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)+A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\varepsilon_{3}\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)

\displaystyle\times\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathcal{D}}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathcal{D}}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}.

Thus for any admissible truncation of
T^{\alpha}
, using the above two
theorems, we obtain

(7.1)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\mathcal{D}}\frac{\left|\left\langle T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathsf{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathcal{D}}f,\mathsf{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathcal{D}}g\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|}{\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathcal{D}}f\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\left\|\mathsf{P}_{\mathop{\mathrm{g}ood}}^{\mathcal{D}}g\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{-s}\left(\omega\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{\kappa,-s}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)+A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)+C\varepsilon_{3}\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right).

Our next task is to use the doubling hypothesis to replace the triple
\kappa
-testing constants by the usual cube testing constants, and we
follow almost verbatim the argument in
[
AlSaUr
]
for the case
s=0
.
Recall that the
\kappa
-cube testing conditions use the
Q
-normalized
monomials
m_{Q}^{\beta}\left(x\right)\equiv\mathbf{1}_{Q}\left(x\right)\left(\frac{x-c_{Q}}{\ell\left(Q\right)}\right)^{\beta}
, for
which we have
\left\|m_{Q}^{\beta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\approx 1
.

Theorem 50
.

Suppose that
\sigma
and
\omega
are locally finite
positive Borel measures on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, with
\sigma
doubling, and
let
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
. If
T^{\alpha}
is a bounded operator from
W^{s}\left(\sigma\right)
to
W^{s}\left(\omega\right)
, then for
every
0<\varepsilon_{2}<1
, there is a positive constant
C\left(\kappa,\varepsilon_{2}\right)
such that

\mathfrak{TR}_{T^{\alpha}}^{\kappa,s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\leq C\left(\kappa,\varepsilon_{2}\right)\left[\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}\right]+\varepsilon_{2}\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\ ,\ \ \ \ \ \kappa\geq 1,

and where the constants
C\left(\kappa,\varepsilon_{2}\right)
depend
only on
\kappa
and
\varepsilon
, and not on the operator norm
\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)
.

Proof.

Fix a dyadic cube
I
. If
P
is an
I
-normalized polynomial of degree less
than
\kappa
on the cube
I
, i.e.
\left\|P\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\approx 1
, then we can approximate
P
by a step function

S\equiv\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(I\right)}a_{I^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{I^{\prime}},

satisfying

\left\|S-\mathbf{1}_{I}P\right\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\sigma\right)}<\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{2}\ ,

provided we take
m\geq 1
sufficiently large depending on
n
and
\kappa
, but independent of the cube
I
. Then using the above lemma with
C2^{\frac{m}{2}}\varepsilon_{1}\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{2}
, and the
estimate
\left|a_{I^{\prime}}\right|\lesssim\left\|P\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I}P\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\leq\left\|\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(I\right)}a_{I^{\prime}}T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I^{\prime}}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}+\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left[\left(S-P\right)\mathbf{1}_{I}\right]\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}\sqrt{\int_{3I}\left|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\left[\left(S-P\right)\mathbf{1}_{I}\right]\right|^{2}d\omega}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(I\right)}\left|a_{I^{\prime}}\right|\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I^{\prime}}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}+\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{I^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(Q\right)}\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sqrt{\left|I^{\prime}\right|_{\sigma}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\leq C\left\{\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\frac{\varepsilon_{2}}{2}\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right\}\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}.

Combining this with (
7.1
) we obtain

\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\leq C\left(\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{-s}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}\right)+C\left(\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3}\right)\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right).

Since
\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)<\infty
for
each truncation, we may absorb the final summand on the right into the left
hand side provided
C\left(\varepsilon_{2}+\varepsilon_{3}\right)<\frac{1}{2}
, to obtain

\mathfrak{N}_{T^{\alpha}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha}}^{s}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\mathfrak{T}_{T^{\alpha,\ast}}^{-s}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}.

By the definition of boundedness of
T^{\alpha}
in (
36
),
this completes the proof of Theorem
2
.

8.
Appendix: necessity of the classical pivotal condition

Here we derive the necessity of the classical
1
-pivotal condition from the
classical
A_{2}
condition and local testing of the vector fractional Riesz
transform with doubling measures. Recall that

\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q,\mu\right)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\ell\left(Q\right)^{1}}{\left(\ell\left(Q\right)+\left|y-c_{Q}\right|\right)^{n+1-\alpha}}d\mu\left(y\right),

and

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\supset\dot{\cup}Q_{r}}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}\ ,

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,\ast}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\supset\dot{\cup}Q_{r}}\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\omega}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q}\omega\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\sigma}=\left(\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\right)^{2}\ .

Recall also the classical fractional Muckenhoupt condition on the measure
pair,

A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\equiv\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}\frac{\left|Q\right|_{\omega}\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|Q\right|^{2\left(1-\frac{\alpha}{n}\right)}}<\infty,

as well as the Sobolev
\mathbf{1}
-testing and
\mathbf{1}^{\ast}
-testing
conditions for the operator
\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}
,

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\sigma}^{\alpha}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W^{s}\left(\omega\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n,s}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\sigma}}\ell\left(I\right)^{-s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n},

\displaystyle\left\|T_{\omega}^{\alpha,\ast}\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\|_{W^{-s}\left(\sigma\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n,-s,\ast}}\left(\omega,\sigma\right)\sqrt{\left|I\right|_{\omega}}\ell\left(I\right)^{s},\ \ \ \ \ I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n},

taken over the family of indicator test functions
\left\{\mathbf{1}_{I}\right\}_{I\in\mathcal{Q}^{n}}
.

Lemma 51
.

For
0\leq\alpha<n
and
\left|s\right|

sufficiently small, we have

\mathcal{V}_{2,s}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n,s}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)+\sqrt{A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)}.

In order to prove this theorem we will model our argument on some of the
material from
[
SaShUr9
]
. We first recall the definition of strong
energy reversal from
[
SaShUr7
]
. We say that a vector
\mathbf{T}^{\alpha}=\left\{T_{\ell}^{\alpha}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{2}
of
\alpha
-fractional transforms in the plane has
strong
reversal of
\omega
-energy on a cube
J
if there is a positive constant
C_{0}
such that for
all
2\leq\gamma\leq 2^{\mathbf{r}\left(1-\varepsilon\right)}
and for
all positive measures
\mu
supported outside
\gamma J
, we have the
inequality

(8.1)

\mathbb{E}_{J}^{\omega}\left[\left(\mathbf{x}-\mathbb{E}_{J}^{\omega}\mathbf{x}\right)^{2}\right]\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\frac{1}{n}}}\right)^{2}=\mathsf{E}\left(J,\omega\right)^{2}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)^{2}\leq C_{0}\ \mathbb{E}_{J}^{\omega}\left|\mathbf{T}^{\alpha}\mu-\mathbb{E}_{J}^{\omega}\mathbf{T}^{\alpha}\mu\right|^{2}.

We now introduce a
stronger
notion of energy reversal which we call
extreme energy reversal. We say that a vector
\mathbf{T}^{\alpha}=\left\{T_{\ell}^{\alpha}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{2}
of
\alpha
-fractional
transforms in the plane has
extreme
reversal of
\omega
-energy on a
cube
J
if there is a Haar function
h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)
and a
positive constant
C_{0}
, such that for all
2\leq\gamma\leq 2^{\mathbf{r}\left(1-\varepsilon\right)}
and for all positive measures
\mu

supported outside
\gamma J
, we have the inequality,

(8.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{J}^{\omega}\left[\left(\mathbf{x}-\mathbb{E}_{J}^{\omega}\mathbf{x}\right)^{2}\right]\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\frac{1}{n}}}\right)^{2}\left|J\right|_{\omega}=\mathsf{E}\left(J,\omega\right)^{2}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)^{2}\left|J\right|_{\omega}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq C\left|\int_{J}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\left[\mathbf{K}^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)-\mathbf{K}^{\alpha}\left(c_{J},y\right)\right]h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2}.

Clearly extreme reversal of energy implies strong reversal of energy.

8.1.
Fractional Riesz transforms

Now we compute for
\beta
real that

\displaystyle\bigtriangleup\left|x\right|^{\beta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\nabla\left|x\right|^{2\frac{\beta}{2}}=\nabla\cdot\left\{\frac{\beta}{2}\left|x\right|^{2\left(\frac{\beta}{2}-1\right)}2x\right\}=\beta\nabla\cdot\left\{x\left|x\right|^{2\frac{\beta-2}{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\left\{\left(\nabla\cdot x\right)\left|x\right|^{2\frac{\beta-2}{2}}+x\cdot\nabla\left|x\right|^{2\frac{\beta-2}{2}}\right\}=\beta\left\{n\left|x\right|^{2\frac{\beta-2}{2}}+x\cdot\frac{\beta-2}{2}\left|x\right|^{2\left(\frac{\beta-2}{2}-1\right)}2x\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\left\{n\left|x\right|^{\beta-2}+\left(\beta-2\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\left|x\right|^{\beta-4}\right\}=\beta\left(n+\beta-2\right)\left|x\right|^{\beta-2}.

The case of interest for us is when
\beta=\alpha-n+1
, since then

(8.3)

\bigtriangleup\left|x\right|^{\beta}=\nabla\cdot\nabla\left|x\right|^{\alpha-n+1}=\nabla\cdot\nabla\left|x\right|^{\alpha-n+1}=c_{\alpha,n}\nabla\cdot\mathbf{K}^{\alpha,n}\left(x\right),

where
\mathbf{K}^{\alpha,n}
is the vector convolution kernel of the
\alpha
-fractional Riesz transform
\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}
. We conclude
that
\bigtriangleup\left|x\right|^{\beta}
is of one sign for
all
x
, provided
\beta\neq 0
and
n+\beta-2\neq 0
, i.e.
\alpha\notin\left\{1,n-1\right\}
. The case
\alpha=1
is not included since
\left|x\right|^{\alpha-n+1}=\left|x\right|^{2-n}
is
the fundamental solution of the Laplacian for
n>2
and constant for
n=2
.
The case
\alpha=n-1
is not included since
\left|x\right|^{\alpha-n+1}=1
is constant.

Thus
z\in J
, we have from (
8.3
) and (
), and with
\mathbf{I}^{\alpha+1,n}\mu\left(z\right)\equiv\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left|z-y\right|^{\alpha+1-n}d\mu\left(y\right)
denoting
the convolution of
\left|x\right|^{\alpha+1-n}
with
\mu
,
that

(8.4)

\left|\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)\right|\gtrsim\left|\mathop{\mathrm{t}race}\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)\right|=\left|\bigtriangleup\mathbf{I}^{\alpha+1,n}\mu\left(z\right)\right|\approx\int\left|y-z\right|^{\alpha-n-1}d\mu\left(y\right)\approx\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)},

where we assume that the positive measure
\mu
is supported outside the
expanded cube
\gamma J
.

Recall that the trace of a matrix is invariant under conjugation by
rotations, and hence is the sum of the eigenvalues of a symmetric matrix. We
now claim that for every
z\in J
, the full matrix gradient
\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)
has at least
1
eigenvalue of size at
least
c\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}
. Indeed, if all eigenvalues of the matrix
\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)
have size at most
c\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}
, then
\left|\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)\right|\leq c\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}
, which
contradicts (
8.4
) if
c
is chosen small enough. This proves our
claim, and moreover, it satisfies the quantitative quadratic estimate

(8.5)

\left|\xi\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)\xi\right|\geq c\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}\left|\xi\right|^{2},\ \ \ \ \ \xi\in\mathsf{S}_{z},\ \text{for }z\in J.

where
\mathsf{S}_{z}\equiv\mathop{\mathrm{S}pan}\mathbf{v}_{z}
, for some
\mathbf{v}_{z}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
. Thus to each
z
in
J
, there corresponds a
unit vector
\mathbf{v}_{z}
for which

\left|\mathbf{v}_{z}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)\mathbf{v}_{z}\right|\geq c\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}.

However, for
w\in J
we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{v}_{z}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)\mathbf{v}_{z}-\mathbf{v}_{z}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(w\right)\mathbf{v}_{z}\right|\leq\left\|\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(z\right)-\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(w\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(\theta_{z,w}\right)\right\|\left|z-w\right|\leq\int\left|\nabla^{2}\mathbf{K}^{\alpha,n}\left(\theta_{z,w},y\right)\right|d\mu\left(y\right)\ \left|z-w\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\frac{1}{\left|\theta_{z,w}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+2}}d\mu\left(y\right)\ \left|z-w\right|=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\frac{1}{\left|\theta_{z,w}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|\theta_{z,w}-y\right|}d\mu\left(y\right)\ \frac{\left|z-w\right|}{\ell\left(J\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\frac{1}{\left|\theta_{z,w}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}d\mu\left(y\right)\ \frac{\left|z-w\right|}{\ell\left(J\right)}\lesssim C_{\gamma}\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)}\frac{\left|z-w\right|}{\ell\left(J\right)},

since
\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|\theta_{z,w}-y\right|}\leq C_{\gamma}
. Thus there is a fixed
m
such that for each
m^{th}

order grandchild
J^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}^{\left(m\right)}\left(J\right)
, we have upon replacing
z
by
c_{J^{\prime}}
in the
displays above,

(8.6)

\left|\mathbf{v}_{c_{J^{\prime}}}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mu\left(w\right)\mathbf{v}_{c_{J^{\prime}}}\right|\geq c\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\ell\left(J\right)},\ \ \ \ \ w\in J^{\prime}\text{,}

i.e. we can use the same unit vector
\mathbf{v}_{c_{J^{\prime}}}
in place
of
v_{z}
for all
z\in J^{\prime}
.

8.2.
Extreme reversal of energy

Lemma 52
.

Let
0\leq\alpha<n
. Then the
\alpha
-fractional
Riesz transform
\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}=\left\{R_{\ell}^{n,\alpha}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{n}
has extreme reversal of
\omega
-energy (
8.2
) on
all
cubes
J
provided
\gamma
is chosen
large enough depending only on
n
and
\alpha
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{J}^{\omega}\left[\left(\mathbf{x}-\mathbb{E}_{J}^{\omega}\mathbf{x}\right)^{2}\right]\left(\frac{\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)}{\left|J\right|^{\frac{1}{n}}}\right)^{2}\left|J\right|_{\omega}=\mathsf{E}\left(J,\omega\right)^{2}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mu\right)^{2}\left|J\right|_{\omega}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \leq C\left|\int_{J}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\mathbf{v}_{c_{J}}\cdot\left[\mathbf{K}^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)-\mathbf{K}^{\alpha}\left(c_{J},y\right)\right]h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2},

where

h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)=\sum_{K\in\mathfrak{C}\left(J\right)}a_{K}\mathbf{1}_{K}\left(x\right)\text{, where }\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}a_{K}>0&\text{ if }&K\text{ lies to the right of center}\\
a_{K}<0&\text{ if }&K\text{ lies to the left of center}\end{array}\right.,

and without loss of generality
\mathbf{v}_{c_{J}}=\mathbf{e}_{1}
. To see
this we compute,

\displaystyle\int_{J}\left[K_{1}^{\alpha,n}\left(x,y\right)-K_{1}^{\alpha,n}\left(c_{J},y\right)\right]h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{J}\left[\frac{x_{1}-y_{1}}{\left|x-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}-\frac{\left(c_{J}\right)_{1}-y_{1}}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\right]h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{J}\left(x_{1}-y_{1}\right)\left\{\frac{1}{\left|x-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}-\frac{1}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\right\}h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)+\int_{J}\left\{\frac{x_{1}-\left(c_{J}\right)_{1}}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\right\}h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle A+B.

Now in term
B
we have
\left(x_{1}-\left(c_{J}\right)_{1}\right)h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)
is of one sign and so

\left|B\right|=\left|\int_{J}\left\{\frac{x_{1}-\left(c_{J}\right)_{1}}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\right\}h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|=\int_{J}\frac{\left|x_{1}-\left(c_{J}\right)_{1}\right|}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\left|h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)\right|d\omega\left(x\right)\geq c\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|},

because
\omega
is doubling. On the other hand,

\displaystyle\left|A\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{J}\left|x_{1}-y_{1}\right|\left|\frac{1}{\left|x-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}-\frac{1}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\right|\left|h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)\right|d\omega\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{\ell\left(J\right)^{2}}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+2}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}=\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\leq C\frac{1}{\gamma}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}

and so for
\gamma>1
chosen sufficiently large, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{J}\left[K_{1}^{\alpha,n}\left(x,y\right)-K_{1}^{\alpha,n}\left(c_{J},y\right)\right]h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\gtrsim

\displaystyle\left|B\right|-\left|A\right|\geq\left(c-C\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{2}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}.

Since
\int_{J}\left[K_{1}^{\alpha,n}\left(x,y\right)-K_{1}^{\alpha,n}\left(c_{J},y\right)\right]h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)
is also of one sign, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{J}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\mathbf{v}_{c_{J}}\cdot\left[\mathbf{K}^{\alpha}\left(x,y\right)-\mathbf{K}^{\alpha}\left(c_{J},y\right)\right]h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\mu\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\left|\int_{J}\left[K_{1}^{\alpha,n}\left(x,y\right)-K_{1}^{\alpha,n}\left(c_{J},y\right)\right]h_{J}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|d\mu\left(y\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\frac{c}{2}\frac{\ell\left(J\right)}{\left|c_{J}-y\right|^{n-\alpha+1}}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}d\mu\left(y\right)=\frac{c}{2}\sqrt{\left|J\right|_{\omega}}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(J,\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma J}\right),

which proves the extreme reversal of energy.

8.3.
Proof of Lemma
51

Proof.

For each
r
we first write
\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q}\sigma\right)=\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\right)+\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\right)
to obtain,

\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}\leq\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}+\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}\ ,

and since
\sigma
is doubling we have
\mathrm{P}_{\kappa}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\right)\approx\frac{\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|Q_{r}\right|^{1-\frac{\lambda}{n}}}
, and hence the first term on the right hand side is
dominated by

\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\sigma}}{\left|Q_{r}\right|^{1-\frac{\lambda}{n}}}\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}\lesssim\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\left(\frac{\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\sigma}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}}{\left|Q_{r}\right|^{2\left(1-\frac{\lambda}{n}\right)}}\right)\leq A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right).

To handle the second term involving
\mathrm{P}_{1}^{\lambda}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\right)
we will
use extreme reversal of energy for the vector
\alpha
-fractional Riesz
transform
\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, which uses the
doubling property of
\omega
.

We first illustrate the constuction by assuming that the unit vector
\mathbf{e}_{1}
in the direction of the positive
x_{1}
-axis, is the
direction
\mathbf{v}_{c_{Q^{r}}}
in which we have the lower bound (
8.6
). Then we fix a special unit Haar function
h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}
, i.e.
\bigtriangleup_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}=h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}
and
\left\|h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}=1
, satisfying

h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)=\sum_{K\in\mathfrak{C}\left(I\right)}a_{K}\mathbf{1}_{K}\left(x\right)\text{, where }\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}a_{K}>0&\text{ if }&K\text{ lies to the right of center}\\
a_{K}<0&\text{ if }&K\text{ lies to the left of center}\end{array}\right.,

where for a cube
Q
centered at the origin, we say a child
K
lies to the
right of center if
K
is contained in the half space where
x_{1}\geq 0
.
In the general case that
\mathbf{v}_{c_{Q^{r}}}
is not equal to
\mathbf{e}_{1}
, we choose a child
Q_{r}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{D}}\left(Q_{r}\right)
of
Q_{r}
that lies to one side of the hyperplane
through
c_{Q^{r}}
with normal vector
\mathbf{v}_{c_{Q^{r}}}
. Then if
Q_{r}^{\prime\prime}
denotes the reflection of
Q_{r}^{\prime}
across
the center
c_{Q^{r}}
of the cube
Q_{r}
, we define

h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)=a_{Q_{r}^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{Q_{r}^{\prime}}\left(x\right)-a_{Q_{r}^{\prime\prime}}\mathbf{1}_{Q_{r}^{\prime\prime}}\left(x\right).

Then we compute,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{1}_{Q_{r}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)-E_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left[\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\right]\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in\mathcal{D}}\ell\left(J\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{J}^{\omega}\left(\mathbf{1}_{Q_{r}}\left(\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\left(x\right)-E_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left[\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\right]\right)\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)^{-2s}\left\|\bigtriangleup_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left(\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\left(x\right)-E_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left[\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)^{-2s}\left|\left\langle\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}},h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\right\rangle_{\omega}\right|^{2}=\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)^{-2s}\left|\int_{Q_{r}}\left(\int_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\mathbf{K}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\left(x,y\right)d\sigma\left(y\right)\right)h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2},

which equals,

\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)^{-2s}\left|\int_{Q_{r}}\int_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\left[\mathbf{K}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\left(x,y\right)-\mathbf{K}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\left(c_{Q_{r}},y\right)\right]h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\sigma\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2}

Since
\omega
is doubling, we have
\mathsf{E}\left(Q_{r},\omega\right)\approx 1
, and from the
extreme energy reversal
inequality (
8.2
), we have

(8.7)

\left|\int_{Q_{r}}\int_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\left[\mathbf{K}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\left(x,y\right)-\mathbf{K}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\left(c_{Q_{r}},y\right)\right]h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\sigma\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2}\gtrsim\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}\ .

With this inequality in hand, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\mathrm{P}^{\alpha}\left(Q_{r},\mathbf{1}_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\right)^{2}\left|Q_{r}\right|_{\omega}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\left|\int_{Q_{r}}\int_{Q\setminus\gamma Q_{r}}\left[\mathbf{K}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\left(x,y\right)-\mathbf{K}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\left(c_{Q_{r}},y\right)\right]h_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left(x\right)d\sigma\left(y\right)d\omega\left(x\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)^{2s}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{Q_{r}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{\gamma Q_{r}}\sigma\left(x\right)-E_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left[\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{\gamma Q_{r}}\right]\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)^{2s}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{Q_{r}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q}\sigma\left(x\right)-E_{Q_{r}}^{\omega}\left[\mathbf{R}_{\sigma}^{\alpha,n}\mathbf{1}_{Q}\right]\right)\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\omega\right)}^{2},

which is dominated by

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\ell\left(Q_{r}\right)^{2s}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{Q_{r}}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}+\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\ell\left(Q\right)^{2s}\left\|\mathbf{1}_{Q}\right\|_{W_{\mathop{\mathrm{d}yad}}^{s}\left(\sigma\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}}\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n,s}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{2}\left|Q\right|_{\sigma}=\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n,s}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{2}.

Thus altogether we have

\mathcal{V}_{2}^{\alpha,1}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{T}_{\mathbf{R}^{\alpha,n,s}}\left(\sigma,\omega\right)^{2}+A_{2}^{\alpha}\left(\sigma,\omega\right),

which completes the proof of Lemma
51
.
